// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-Machines]]
= Unprivileged Architecture: Machines
ifdef::env-site[]
// On the site each page stands alone; the PDF gets these from src/Zkl.adoc.
include::partial$Zkl-symbols.adoc[]
endif::[]

_This chapter is normative._

All values, bit slices, concatenations, and finite-field operations in this chapter follow the conventions of <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>. Specifically, the byte at the lowest address occupies the least significant bits, and the `@` operator places its left operand in the more significant bits, opposite to `{vvert}`, which places its left operand at the lower offsets (<<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation-operators>>). No Machine may deviate from these conventions.

This chapter specifies KLE instruction behavior for supported Machines. Some Machines are defined generically for primitives meeting certain properties (e.g., GCM works with AES or SM4), while others are tied to specific primitives (e.g., Ascon).

<<KLE-exec-encodings>> gives the encoding of each Machine of this specification in the MDH field _Machine_,
which has two sub-fields: _Type_ in bits [11:4] and _Mode_ in bits [3:0].
For some classes of Machines, _Mode_ specifies a subtype;
other groups of Machines may subdivide _Machine_ differently.

//

== Definition of a Machine in KLE

The following information fully defines a Machine:

Parameters::
+
--
These include:

* the _block size_ `b`, the bit length of the basic unit of information that the Machine processes, usually the block size of its main underlying primitive;
* the _key size_ `k`, also in bits, for Machines that hold a key;
* the _granularity_, usually equal to `b`. “Last blocks”, such as those of the AD or of the plaintext or ciphertext, may be shorter than `b`. Where the length of such a block is set explicitly, a longer input that carries it is truncated: its excess most significant bits are ignored; and
* any other parameter needed to instantiate the Machine with compatible primitives.
--

Data Structures::
+
--
These include:

. *Provisioning Input*:
its fields, in the order in which they appear in the PI,
each with its length.
The PI is implicitly zero-padded at the end to a multiple of 128 bits; provisioning ignores the padding bits.
+
Every PI starts with the MDH, at position i.
The *Provisioning Input* tables of the Machines omit it and start at position ii:
+
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|   i  | 128           | MDH
|===

. *Internal State*:
the information kept in the internal state of a CC, which is needed to define the Machine and its semantics.
It must exist internally, but its representation and format are not prescribed.
The Internal State always contains the MDH.
A key field of the Internal State holds the key itself, even when the PI or the SCC carries a System Key Identifier in its place (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-system-keys>>).

. *Serialized Content*:
the plaintext of the `Content1` of the SCC:
its fields, in the order in which they appear in an SCC, each with its length.
It is implicitly zero-padded at the end to a multiple of 128 bits,
and does not include the MDH.
--

Machine-Specific Functions::
Any other function that is specific to the Machine.

State Machine::
+
--
The state machine is defined by:

. the list of the *States* that the Machine may assume, each with its representation as <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-State-field>> specifies;
. the complete list of *allowed state transitions*, with the prerequisites of each, the native `kl.setst` Form and the meaning of any auxiliary parameter to it, and any other condition that triggers a transition without a `kl.setst`; and
. the *allowed* or *expected* instructions in each State, and their semantics.
  Besides configuration and export sequences, a State typically permits only certain Forms of `kl.exec` and `kl.setst`, and may be an endpoint of `kl.derive` (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>).
With `Zklio`, a substitution may replace any native Form, however the Machine words it
(<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-usage-input-output>>, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-substitution>>):
a Form D `kl.exec` replaces a Form A, B or C `kl.exec`, and a Form A `kl.setst` replaces a Form C `kl.setst`.
A substitution issued without the required KLIOBUF setup fails as Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-substitution-failures>> states.
A Form that is neither a native Form of the State nor, with `Zklio`, its substitution is a wrong Form (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-form-mismatch>>).
`kl.input` and `kl.output` access no locker, so the state machine does not restrict them.
Rule <<KLE-MGR-not-allowed-instructions>> in <<KLE-Machine-rules>> states the behavior precisely.
--

//

The non-normative code examples given in <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-examples>>
illustrate how software uses the Machines through the KLE ISA;
the examples use the vector Forms of the instructions.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-algo-notation]]
=== Notation in the Machine Descriptions

The following nomenclature is specific to the Machine specifications:

* `INPUT` is the input data of `kl.exec` or `kl.setst`: the input vector register,
  or the KLIOBUF (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-usage-input-output>>).

* `OUTPUT` is the output data of `kl.exec`: the output
  vector register, or the KLIOBUF (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-usage-input-output>>).

* `Xs` is the auxiliary operand of a Form B `kl.setst`: a 64-bit value, in one GPR on RV64 and in the pair `X[s+1] @ X[s]` on RV32 (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-multiple-GPRs>>).
* `KLLEN` is the operand length in bits: `vl` {times} `SEW` for a vector operand, and
  `kliobuftop` {times} 8 for a KLIOBUF operand. `kliobuftop`, like every
  other KLIOBUF CSR and like `klstart`, is a number of bytes.
  For a KLIOBUF operand, `kliobuftop` plays the role of `VL`, and the transfer
  window is bytes [0, `kliobuftop`-1] (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-CSR-kliobuftop>>).
  For either operand of `kl.exec`, `klstart` plays the role of `vstart`: it is the byte
  position within the transfer window at which processing starts or resumes
  (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-CSR-klstart>>).
  `kl.setst` does not honor `klstart`: a native Form C one reads its whole operand, and a substituted one reads bytes
  [0, `kliobuftop`-1] (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-usage-input-output>>).

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-Machine-rules]]
== General Rules for Machines

Besides the General Rules (GR) of <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-unpriv>>, in particular those governing the _State_ field and its transitions (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-State-management>>), the following Machine Rules (*MR*) apply to all Machines, unless stated otherwise.

[horizontal]
[[KLE-MGR-not-allowed-instructions,MR1]]MR1:: A usage instruction, or a Form of it, that may modify the state of a locker and that the current State does
not allow transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_. It raises no illegal-instruction exception: only the grounds
of <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-exceptions-list>> do, such as a reserved encoding or an unimplemented extension.
+
`kl.clearads` is allowed in every Valid State (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>); a `kl.setst` naming _Success_ or
_Failure_ in none (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-success-failure-cannot-be-set-directly>>).
A wrong Form, including a substitution that the State does not admit or that is issued without `Zklio`, is not allowed
(Rules <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-substitution-needs-zklio>> and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-form-mismatch>>).
`kl.setste`, which names an Error State, `kl.clear` and `kl.clearall` are not usage instructions
(<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-properties>>), so no State restricts them.

[[KLE-MGR-granularity,MR2]]MR2:: If `KLLEN` violates the granularity constraints of the Machine in its current State, the instruction performs no operation and the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_, with its output window zeroed
(Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-zero-unwritten-output>>).
The granularity constrains the transfers of whole blocks, for instance to a whole multiple of the block size.
In a State that processes a final block or piece of `last_block_len` bits, the constraint is `KLLEN` {ge} `last_block_len`.
A fixed-width input follows Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>, and the Machine defines the constraint on a tag that it admits truncated.

[[KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block,MR3]]MR3:: _Single- and Multi-block `kl.exec`._
In a State that processes whole blocks of `b` bits, one `kl.exec` applies the per-block operation of the State
`KLLEN`/`b` times, for `i` = 0, `b`, `2b`, {ellipsis}, `KLLEN` − `b` in that order, consuming `INPUT[i+b-1:i]` if the State
takes an input and producing `OUTPUT[i+b-1:i]` if it produces an output. Blocks are thus taken and produced from the least
significant position upwards, here and throughout this specification.
A resumed `kl.exec` performs only the iterations from `i` = 8 {interpunct} `klstart` onwards (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-CSR-klstart>>).
+
A State, or a State with a _StateExtension_ flag set, that the Machine describes as processing a single block, such as a
last-block, tag-finalize or verify State, or XEX _Decrypt_ during ciphertext stealing (<<KLE-XEX-XTS-modes>>), processes
exactly one block whatever `KLLEN` is, unless its transfer window is invalid (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>) or empty (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-transfer-window-checks>>).
Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>> governs a further `kl.exec` in a last-block State.
The `last_block_state` of <<KLE-process-VLI>> instead processes exactly its final piece, which may first complete the current block.
The only interruption point of a `kl.exec` in such a State, or of one that processes a final piece, is 0
(Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-interruption-point>>).

[[KLE-MGR-recomputed-fields,MR4]]MR4:: A field that a Machine can derive inexpensively
from other fields of the same CC may be omitted from the Serialized Content.
It is then recomputed when an import completes, by the `kl.mgmt` with `#kl_cfg_management_end`, whatever the format,
a system-specific one (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-completion>>) included.
It is also recomputed whenever a field it depends on is modified, for instance when `kl.derive` overwrites a key field,
or when an explicit operation of the Machine regenerates a field it depends on.
A Machine states only the derivation itself, and marks such a field “(derived)” in its *Internal State*.

[[KLE-MGR-atomic-randomness,MR5]]MR5:: _Randomness:_
+
--
* A Machine operation that needs random bits from an RBG or DRBG, such as the generation of a nonce, an ephemeral scalar or a signature into the locker, is an instruction of its own.
  That instruction works only on the locker and on scalar GPR operands captured at issue, transfers no data to or from memory, vector registers, the KLIOBUF or another locker, and may be long-running (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-no-data>>).
  If the RBG or DRBG cannot supply the bits, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-rbg-failure>> applies.
* An instruction that transfers data never waits or fails for lack of random bits, even when resumed, restored or restarted; further randomness that its Machine needs comes from another instruction.
* Randomness that the implementation needs, such as masks, is supplied in advance, without weakening the required protection.
* An internal algorithmic random value, such as a signing nonce or an ephemeral scalar, serves only the one instance of the scheme that requires it, and no other instance or purpose.
  It is never reused, not even when an interrupted operation restarts (Rule <<KLE-MGR-progress-discard>>).
  Resuming copies of one interrupted operation, restored from one image, completes that one instance on the same inputs and is not a reuse.
--

[[KLE-MGR-progress-discard,MR6]]MR6:: _Implementation-defined interruption of long-running operations._
A State in which a `kl.exec` may be interrupted under Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-preemption>> admits only that instruction and Form, the `kl.setst` instructions that its Machine allows in the State, `kl.clearads` (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>) and configuration instructions.
Each such State designates a progress field _P_, non-zero from the first precise halt of the operation until its completion, and zero otherwise.
The random and intermediate values that the operation keeps in a state field or in the ADS, its _material_, are meaningful only while _P_ is non-zero:

. If _P_ is non-zero, the instruction resumes the operation, or restarts it after zeroing _P_, and a restart generates the material anew.
. An interrupt leaves _P_ and the material unchanged.
. If _P_ is non-zero, `kl.clearads` and every transition of the locker's _State_, a same-State `kl.setst` included, zero _P_ and destroy the material.
The moves of a management operation into and out of a Configuration State are not such transitions (Rule <<KLE-MGR-management-no-state-operations>>).
. An export or an import zeroes _P_ and destroys the material only where it restarts the operation (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-restart>>).
A `kl.clone` always restarts the operation in the copy, zeroing its _P_ and destroying its material; the source keeps both.
. When the operation completes, _P_ is zeroed, and the algorithm of the Machine determines whether the material is kept.
. A long-running `kl.setst` (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-long-running-no-data>>) is not resumable: on preemption it restarts
under option 5 of Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-resumability-options>>, keeping no _P_ and no material, and draws its random values
again (Rule <<KLE-MGR-atomic-randomness>>).

[[KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input,MR7]]MR7:: Where a State consumes from `INPUT` a single value of fixed width, that of the field it assigns or of the
operand it is defined over, the width need not be the block size `b`.
If `KLLEN` exceeds that width, only that many least significant bits of `INPUT` are used, and the rest are ignored.
A smaller `KLLEN` violates the granularity of the State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).

[[KLE-MGR-clear-output,MR8]]MR8:: A State writes `OUTPUT` only within its output window, bits [8 {times} `klstart`, `KLLEN` − 1] (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-zero-unwritten-output>>).
An output longer than `KLLEN` is truncated to its `KLLEN` least significant bits, unless it is meant to be emitted over
several `kl.exec`, as a long field (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>) or a digest or XOF output is; a `KLLEN` below a minimum that the Machine sets violates the
granularity of the State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).
When a State writes fewer bits than the window holds, it clears the remaining bits of the window.
This concerns the bits *within* the operand; the tail-agnostic policy of <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-KLV>>
governs the elements beyond `VL`.

[[KLE-MGR-load-long-field,MR9]]MR9:: _Long fields transferred by several `kl.exec`._
A State _X_ that loads a long field from `INPUT`, or emits one to `OUTPUT`, over several `kl.exec` names a state field _W_, which records the amount transferred so far, and its unit, bits or bytes.
Where _W_ is a field of the MDH, such as _MachineUse_, `kl.restrict*` cannot write it (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-restrict>>).
A digest or XOF output that a State emits from its internal state, computed on entering the State or as it is emitted, is not a long field: its Machine defines the progress and the outcome of an excess `KLLEN`, as <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>> does.

. *Entry.* Entering _X_ zeroes _W_; entering a loading State also zeroes the field.
. *Progress.* _W_ rises by the amount transferred: for a `kl.exec` that completes, by `KLLEN` − 8 {interpunct} `klstart` bits or `KLLEN`/8 − `klstart` bytes, up to the size of the field; for one that halts, by its committed prefix (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-precise-halt>>), so that its resumption adds only the remainder.
. *Completion.* The transfer is complete once _W_ reaches the size of the field.
  Completion does not reset _W_, but a transition that the Machine makes then zeroes it.
  Unless the Machine defines another transition, an emitting State then transitions to _Success_, and a loading State admits no further `kl.exec` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-not-allowed-instructions>>).
. *Excess.* Excess data in the final load is ignored; an emitting `kl.exec` that would carry _W_ past the field size transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. *Incomplete load.* A `kl.setst` that leaves a loading State _X_ before the load is complete leaves the field unconfigured: it zeroes the field and any length of it set on entering _X_, and leaves its flag, if any, clear.
  A same-State `kl.setst` (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>) restarts the load: as on entering _X_, it zeroes _W_ and the field, and clears its flag, if any.
  `kl.clearads` does not leave _X_.
  A Machine whose next State needs the field states this as an entry condition (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
. *Flags.* A Machine may keep in _StateExtension_ a flag per loaded field that records a configured value, such as the `Has` flags of the ECC, ML-KEM and ML-DSA Machines and the `HasKey` flag of HMAC.
  Unless the Machine states otherwise, entering the loading State clears the flag, and a load that completes and passes the checks of Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>, or an operation of the Machine that computes the field, sets it; `kl.getstx` reads it.
  Each Machine lists its flags, their bit positions and any departure from this pattern.
. *`PROCESS_VLI`.* A load by `PROCESS_VLI` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>) passes `max_len` = the size of the field and `cumul_len` = _W_.
  A field stored as loaded also passes `block` = `state`, `b` = `n` = its size, `mode` = `assign` and `process_block` = `finalize` = `None`; a field processed as loaded, such as the IV of <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, passes the functions and `mode` that its Machine states.

[[KLE-MGR-single-last-block,MR10]]MR10:: _Single final block._
A State that processes a single final block, such as _Enc_Last_Block_, _Dec_Last_Block_ or _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, admits at most one `kl.exec` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>).
Unless the Machine defines a transition of its own on its completion, the locker stays in the State.
A further `kl.exec` then transitions it to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-not-allowed-instructions>>), with its output window zeroed (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-zero-unwritten-output>>).
An empty final input, such as an empty final block of the AD of OCB (<<KLE-OCB-mode>>), needs and admits no `kl.exec` that transfers data: a `kl.exec` with a non-empty transfer window (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-transfer-window-checks>>) transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_, with its output window zeroed (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-zero-unwritten-output>>).
A Machine may still require a native Form D `kl.exec`, which has no transfer window, as _Enc_Last_Block_ and _Dec_Last_Block_ of <<KLE-OCB-mode>> do.
+
NOTE: A second final block is not a continuation of the input but an attempt to manipulate the internal state of the Machine.

[[KLE-MGR-policy-gate,MR11]]MR11:: _Policy gate and entry conditions._
+
--
Each Machine states, for each of its States:

* the bit of _MachinePolicy_ (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Machine-field>>), if any, that enables its operation; in a Machine, “if encryption is allowed” refers to _MachinePolicy_[0] and “if decryption is allowed” to _MachinePolicy_[1].
A bit gates the States of its operation and every State that follows uniquely from them, its _Gated States_:
** encryption: _Encrypt_, _Enc_Last_Block_, _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ and, where it emits the tag of the encryption, _Hash_Output_;
** decryption: _Decrypt_, _Dec_Last_Block_, _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ and, in <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, <<KLE-OCB-mode>> and <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, _Hash_Verify_;
** signature generation: _Sign_Generate_, _Output_ with a signature, and _Sign_Output_;
** signature verification: _Sign_Input_ and _Sign_Verify_;
** MAC generation and verification: _Hash_Output_ and _Hash_Verify_, respectively; and
* its _entry conditions_: what the State requires, such as the `Has` flags, the mode, and the other configuration of the CC that it uses.

Unless the Machine states otherwise, a zero _MachinePolicy_ is:

* invalid Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>) for a Machine that encrypts and decrypts, or that generates and verifies MACs;
* valid for a Machine that generates and verifies signatures: it disables these operations and leaves the other operations of the Machine, if any, available.

A `kl.exec`, a `kl.derive`, or a `kl.setst` that is a usage instruction (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-properties>>) is checked:

. *At issue, against _MachinePolicy_.* The locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_, and the instruction performs no operation, if the instruction:
.. is issued in a State whose operation _MachinePolicy_ disables, or from which every operation that the Machine can complete is disabled, unless Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>> admits it or it is a `kl.setst` naming a State that still leads to an enabled operation; or
.. would enter a State whose operation _MachinePolicy_ disables, by naming it or on its completion (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>).
. *After it acts, against the entry conditions of the State that it enters.* They are checked after the operations of leaving the current State, on the content that these operations and the instruction produce, such as the last part of the IV that the completing `kl.exec` of <<KLE-GCM-mode>> writes; if they do not hold, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ instead of entering the State.

Within a State, an instruction checks a condition only if the condition can change in that State, or if the check is one of Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>; the Machine states the check with the instruction, and a failed check transitions the locker to _Invalid_ unless it detects a data error (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>).
--

[[KLE-MGR-config-data-error,MR12]]MR12:: _Configuration errors and data errors._
Each Machine states which of its checks detect a configuration error and which a data error:

* A _configuration error_ transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_. It is either:
** a field that sets a parameter or a key of the CC, not the data of an operation, with a value that the referenced standard rejects, such as a malformed private key; or
** a field, a peer point or a signature included, whose encoding violates a constraint of its representation that is checked when the field is written, such as the zero most significant bits of secp521r1 (<<KLE-ECC>>).
A Machine may classify such a check as a data error instead, as <<KLE-PQC-ML-KEM>> does for the input checks of cite:[nist-fips-203] on `encapsk` and `ciphertext`.
A constraint that the referenced standard checks only when an operation decodes the value, such as the hint encoding of an ML-DSA `signature` (cite:[nist-fips-204] Algorithm 21), is a data error, detected by that operation.
* A _data error_ transitions the locker to _Failure_, a Valid State. It is either:
** a ciphertext, tag, signature, message representative or public key that is malformed, does not verify, or fails an input check that the Machine classifies as a data error, such as the check of an ML-KEM encapsulation key; or
** a point that an operation uses, such as a peer's point, a public key or a generator, that is not a valid point of the curve.

[[KLE-MGR-check-on-write,MR13]]MR13:: _Checks of a field._
A check that the Machine attaches to a field and that depends on that field alone is made whenever the field is written: at the completion of its load (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>), of a `kl.derive` into it, of the provisioning that carries it, and of an import that restores it.
Examples are the zero most significant bits of secp521r1, the well-formedness of an ML-DSA `privkey`, and the input checks of cite:[nist-fips-203] on an ML-KEM key.
Such a check need not be repeated when the field is read, since the KLF guarantees the integrity of the locker (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-KLF>>).
A Machine may repeat it on a value that an interrupted operation resumes with, as secp521r1 does (<<KLE-ECC>>); a repeated check that fails transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
A check that depends on the operation or on more than one field is made by the operation that reads the field, before it changes any state.
Examples are the validity of a point in the role that the operation gives it, the range of `Scalar` against the group order, and the consistency of a public key with a private key.
The validity of a point is thus not checked when the point is written.
Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>> classifies the failure of any check other than a repeated one.

[[KLE-MGR-tag-verify,MR14]]MR14:: _Verification of a tag._
A Machine that verifies a tag or a MAC without disclosing the value it computes performs the verification in constant time.
It then transitions the state machine to _Success_ if the verification succeeds, and to _Failure_ otherwise (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>).
The instruction that provides the tag or MAC to verify performs the verification. This instruction is:

* by default, a Form C `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Verify_, whose auxiliary operand carries the expected value;
* where a `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Verify_ sets the number of bits to compare instead, as for CMAC, HMAC and KMAC (<<KLE-CMAC-mode>>, <<KLE-HMAC>>, <<KLE-KMAC>>), or where the Machine enters _Hash_Verify_ by itself, as OCB and Ascon-AEAD128 do (<<KLE-OCB-mode>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>), a single Form B `kl.exec` in _Hash_Verify_, which supplies the expected value;
* where the expected value is supplied first, as the SIV of GCM-SIV is (<<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>>), the `kl.exec` that completes the internal tag computation.

[[KLE-MGR-sysfmt-standard,MR15]]MR15:: _System-specific import._
A system-specific import (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-diligence>>) is never a way around a requirement of the referenced standard of the Machine.
The converted Content undergoes the checks of Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>, and a field that the Machine derives from another is derived from the converted Content, or checked against it, never taken from the format unchecked.
A Machine whose Provisioning Input does not carry a field that a format may configure states how the import checks and marks that field, as <<KLE-PQC-ML-KEM>> and <<KLE-PQC-ML-DSA>> do.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-truncation-vs-length]]
== Input and Output Padding, Truncation, Byte Granularity

Unless the Machine specifies otherwise and pads internally, the caller must pad an input whose length the granularity of the State does not admit to a `KLLEN` that the Machine admits, and pass its actual length to the Machine, typically through `kl.setst`.

Cryptographic inputs are normally supplied through vector registers or the KLIOBUF, in whole bytes. For a standard that permits arbitrary bit lengths, the caller specifies the bit length of the final input piece, usually as a `kl.setst` parameter, and the Machine truncates that input to it. The Machine clears the output bits beyond the final piece, also within its last byte (Rule <<KLE-MGR-clear-output>>).

This occurs, for instance, with the `last_block_len` parameters in <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>>, <<KLE-OCB-mode>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>, and <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>. Such a parameter gives the length to which the Machine truncates the passed input, not the length `KLLEN` of that input. Macro `PROCESS_VLI` receives field lengths in bits, but `kl.exec` always transfers whole bytes, and the last input may be truncated internally.

Where a standard's formula requires the bit count of data that the Machine receives in whole bytes, it is the byte count multiplied by 8, as with the length block of GHASH in <<KLE-GCM-mode>>.

[horizontal]
[[KLE-MGR-last-blk-len,MR16]]MR16::
+
--
A `last_block_len`, or any other parameter giving the bit length of a final input piece, is a multiple of 8, unless the Machine either:

* states that its referenced standard orders the bits within a byte as <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>> does, so that a truncation of the last byte to its least significant bits is the bit string that the standard defines (as for <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>); or
* defines which bits of the partial final byte carry the piece (as for the nonce of <<KLE-OCB-mode>>).

A value that the Machine does not admit transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
Whether zero is admissible depends on the Machine.
--

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

// [[KLE-DRBG]]
// == Deterministic Random Bit Generator
//
// This Machine defines a deterministic random bit generator (DRBG) that can be used to generate random numbers.
// It must be based on a NIST SP 800-90A Rev. 1 cite:[nist-SP-800-90-r1] and correspond to a Security Strength
// of 256 bits.
//
// The *Provisioning Input* consists only of the MDH.
//
// The *Internal State* may contain all the necessary data for the DRBG to function.
//
// The *Serialized Content* consists only of the MDH.
//
// State Machine::
//
// * *States:*
//  ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
//
// * *Allowed State Transitions:*
//  ** None.
//
// * *Behavior:*
//  ** When the locker is provisioned or an SCC is imported into it, the internal state of the Machine obtains new true random bits from the underlying RNG and is accordingly initialized.
//  ** This is the only algorithm that allows ``kl.exec`` instructions in State _Ready_.
//     In State _Ready_, (multi-block) ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected to be of Form C.
//     "``kl.exec OUTPUT, Kn``" produces KLLEN bytes of pseudo-random bits.
//
// Since the SCC of the DRBG Machine consists of the MDH only, and the DRBG is reset at each import,
// the export and import routines are the same as for lockers in an Error State, i.e., there is no authentication.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-states-symmetric]]
== Symmetric Cryptography

This table, with <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-state-off>>, <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-states-valid>> and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-stateexts-error>>,
defines the States of the symmetric Machines.
A Machine may override these values and says so where it does; the public-key Machines define their own mnemonics.

[[KLE-state-constants-symmetric]]
.Common Constants defining CC states for Symmetric Machines
[float="center",align="center",width="100%",cols="^7%,<30%,<17%,<46%",options="header"]
|===
| Value | Mnemonic for the Constant        | State Name          | Description
.2+| 2  |  `kl_state_operate`              |  _Operate_          | Generic operational state.
<|  `kl_state_hash_absorb`                 | _Hash_Absorb_       | (Same value as _Operate_, with a mnemonic more intuitive for hash functions.)
Absorb data: the message of a hash function, MAC or XOF, or the associated data of an AEAD scheme; on the encryption path of <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>>, also the plaintext. On entering the State, the Machine may initialize its tag or MAC computation.
|   3   |  `kl_state_hash_last_block`      | _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_   | Absorb a final input piece whose length the `kl.setst` sets, such as the last partial block of the associated data or of a MAC input, or the final piece of a hashed message, of any bit length that the standard admits.
|   4   |  `kl_state_hash_finalize`        | _Hash_Finalize_     | Finalize the internal state of a hash/tag function.
|   5   |  `kl_state_hash_verify`          | _Hash_Verify_       | Compare the internal hash/tag value to the provided one.
|   6   |  `kl_state_hash_output`          | _Hash_Output_       | Enter the hash output phase of a hash/tag function.
|   7   |  `kl_state_encrypt`              | _Encrypt_           | Perform encryption, and any associated tag computation.
|   8   |  `kl_state_decrypt`              | _Decrypt_           | Perform decryption, and any associated tag computation.
|   9   |  `kl_state_enc_last_block`       | _Enc_Last_Block_    | Process the last block separately, on the encryption path of an authenticated encryption mode.
|  10   |  `kl_state_dec_last_block`       | _Dec_Last_Block_    | Process the last block separately, on the decryption path of an authenticated encryption mode.
|  11   |  `kl_state_enc_tag_finalize`     | _Enc_Tag_Finalize_  | Finalize the tag computation for AE mode encryption.
|  12   |  `kl_state_dec_tag_finalize`     | _Dec_Tag_Finalize_  | Finalize the internal tag computation for AE mode decryption; where the expected tag was supplied first, as in <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>>, also verify it (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>).
|  13   |  `kl_state_set_aux_value`        | _Set_Aux_Value_     | Set a public/auxiliary value in a cryptographic scheme implemented by the Machine.
|  14   |  `kl_state_set_aux_value_2`      | _Set_Aux_Value_2_   | Set a second public/auxiliary value in a cryptographic scheme implemented by the Machine.
|  15   |  `kl_state_set_key`              | _Set_Key_           | Reconfigure a key field.
|===

Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>> applies to the Machines that use
<<KLE-state-constants-symmetric>> and to every Machine-defined immediate; Rules <<KLE-MGR-management-no-state-operations>> to <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>
apply to every Machine.

[horizontal]
// Subsumed by Rule GR16, under which a same-State kl.setst is not admitted unless allowed:
// [[KLE-MGR-no-hash-output-repeat,MR17]]MR17:: A `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Output_ issued while the locker is in that State
// transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>).

[[KLE-operation-immediates,MR17]]MR17:: A Machine may declare a `kl.setst` an _operation_ if it names a constant of <<KLE-state-constants-symmetric>> or a
Machine-defined immediate, 66–127 (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-instruction-setst>>).
An operation performs the stated action and leaves _State_ unchanged; it changes _StateExtension_ only where the Machine
states so.
It is not a transition: it performs none
of the operations of leaving or entering a State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-management-no-state-operations>>), and
Rules <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>, <<KLE-MGR-progress-discard>> and <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>> do not treat it
as one.
The Machine states the States in which the operation is allowed, and its Form; in any other State, a constant of <<KLE-state-constants-symmetric>> names a State, and a Machine-defined immediate is not admitted (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-setst-not-admitted>>).
No Machine declares an operation in a State of Rule <<KLE-MGR-progress-discard>>.
_Set_Aux_Value_, for instance, is an operation in <<KLE-keystream-modes>>, <<KLE-tweakable>> and <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>,
and a State in <<KLE-GCM-mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>> and <<KLE-OCB-mode>>; `kl_xex_state_steal` is an operation in
_Decrypt_ of <<KLE-XEX-XTS-modes>>.

[[KLE-MGR-management-no-state-operations,MR18]]MR18:: The operations that a Machine performs on entering or leaving a State, or on a change of State, apply only to the transitions between its Valid States that usage instructions make.
A `kl.setst` naming _Ready_ issued in _Ready_ is such a transition: it performs the operations of entering _Ready_.
A management operation's moves into and out of a Configuration State perform none of them,
and neither does the restoring of the saved State when an export or an import completes.
Two events other than such a transition also perform the operations of entering _Ready_:
+
--
* the completion of a provisioning, or of a system-specific import unless its format defines other initialization operations in their place (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-completion>>); and
* a `kl.derive` that writes a key in _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-checks>> item 5).
--

[[KLE-MGR-caller-limits,MR19]]MR19:: A Machine enforces only the per-message limits that its correctness needs at practical lengths, such as the
32-bit counters of <<KLE-GCM-mode>> and <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>> and the bound on the `L[i]` array of <<KLE-OCB-mode>>.
The caller must keep every other limit; no Machine counts or enforces it:
+
--
* the length of a message is limited by the referenced standard, such as the 2^20^ blocks of a data unit of
  cite:[nist-SP-800-38E], and by the width of a field that records it, such as the 64 bits of `cumul_len`,
  a width that this specification chooses and that no message reaches in practice; beyond the latter limit, the digest or
  tag that the Machine produces is unspecified, but it is computed from the Content and the message alone and is not
  otherwise secret Content (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-no-extraction>>);
* the use of a key across messages is limited as the referenced standard states, for instance in the total data processed,
  the number of failed verifications, or a parameter fixed for the lifespan of the key.
  The limit applies across every locker and SCC that holds the key, including its copies by `kl.clone` and its re-imports;
* the uniqueness of a nonce, IV, counter or tag mask, as the referenced standard requires: no Machine prevents its reuse,
  whether by re-importing an earlier SCC, by `kl.clone` or by returning to _Ready_, even when the IV or nonce is set at
  provisioning.
--

[[KLE-MGR-machine-transitions,MR20]]MR20:: _Transitions that a Machine makes._
A Machine may make a transition by itself: on completing a `kl.exec`, or the part of a `kl.derive` that acts at an
endpoint as that `kl.exec` would (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-derive-rule-both-fixed-size>>), or within a `kl.setst`
that names another State.
Such a transition is made only that way: no source State allows a `kl.setst` naming its destination
(Rule <<KLE-MGR-not-allowed-instructions>>), unless the destination is _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>)
or the Machine lists that `kl.setst`.
Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>> gates such a transition as it gates a `kl.setst`.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-ECB-mode]]
=== Electronic Codebook Modes (Direct Encryption/Decryption)

ECB mode cite:[nist-SP-800-38A-1] is the simplest way to use a block cipher.
(((KLE, Machine, ECB)))

Parameters::

* `b` = bit length of a block (8|`b`), with `b` {ge} 64.
* `k` = bit length of a key (8|`k`).
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::
* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64      | `key` or System Key Identifier
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  i    | `k` or 64      | `key` or System Key Identifier
|===
--

Machine-Specific Functions::
* `enc_blk(K,p) -> c` encrypts plaintext block `p` using the key `K`, and
  returns the ciphertext result `c`.
* `dec_blk(K,c) -> p` decrypts ciphertext block `c` using the key `K`, and
  returns the plaintext result `p`.

State Machine::

* *States:*
 ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
 ** _Encrypt_: Encrypting blocks of input.
 ** _Decrypt_: Decrypting blocks of input.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Encrypt_, if encryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Decrypt_, if decryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** The `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ is of Form A: it takes no auxiliary operand.
    The Machine allows a same-State `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>), which changes nothing.
 ** In _Encrypt_ and _Decrypt_, (multi-block) Form A `kl.exec` instructions are expected.
    For each block, `OUTPUT <- enc_blk(key, INPUT)` in _Encrypt_, and `OUTPUT <- dec_blk(key, INPUT)` in _Decrypt_.

//

The code examples in <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-ECB-encryption>> and
<<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-ECB-encryption-multi-block>> illustrate how to use an ECB CC to encrypt data, one
block or eight blocks per `kl.exec`.

// // ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-chaining]]
=== Chaining/Feedback Modes

KLE does not architect chaining modes, such as Cipher Block Chaining (CBC), Cipher Feedback (CFB) and Output Feedback (OFB) cite:[nist-SP-800-38A-1].
Software builds them on ECB: each intermediate value needs no more protection than the plaintext that software already holds.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-keystream-modes]]
=== CTR/XCTR Keystream Generation

In CTR mode, the IV and the counter are concatenated to form the input block of the underlying keyed PRP or PRF, so their lengths sum to its block size.
(((KLE, Machine, keystream generation)))

In XCTR mode, the IV has the block size; the counter is zero-extended to the block size, and its XOR with the IV forms the input block.

Parameters::
* `b` = block size (8|`b`), with `b` {ge} 64.
* `k` = key size (8|`k`).
* `j` = counter size: 32 in CTR mode, 64 in XCTR mode.
* `n` = IV size (8|`n`).
* In CTR mode, `b = n + j`; in XCTR mode, `b = n`.
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::
* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits.
 ** `IV`: `n` bits.
 ** `ctr`: `j` bits.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|    i  | `k` or 64       | `key` or System Key Identifier
|   ii  | `n`             | `IV`
|  iii  | `j`             | `ctr`
|===
--

Machine-Specific Functions::
* `keystream_block(p) -> c`: `enc_blk(key, p)` (<<KLE-ECB-mode>>), the encryption of the counter block `p` under `key`.
* `tick_ctr()`: increases `ctr` by one, modulo 2^`j`^.

State Machine::
* *States:*
  ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
  ** _Operate_: The CC produces a keystream.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ or _Operate_ -> _Operate_, if encryption or decryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Operate_ -> _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** When entering _Ready_, `IV` {leftarrow} 0, and `ctr` {leftarrow} 0 in CTR mode and 1 in XCTR mode
    (as in the XCTR of HCTR2 cite:[cryptoeprint-2021-1441]).

 ** When entering _Operate_, the Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_operate` and auxiliary operand `INPUT`
    sets `IV` {leftarrow} `INPUT`, with `INPUT` truncated to its `n` least significant bits
    (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>), and leaves `ctr` unchanged.
    The Machine allows a same-State `kl.setst` into _Operate_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>), which
    does the same: it replaces `IV` and keeps `ctr`.

 ** In State _Operate_, (multi-block) Form C ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected.
    For each block, they perform:
+
--
  . In CTR mode, `tmp <- keystream_block(bswap(ctr) @ IV)`;
    in XCTR mode, `tmp <- keystream_block(IV xor (zeros(b - j) @ ctr))`.
  . `tick_ctr()`.
  . `OUTPUT <- tmp`.
--
+
As a `kl.derive` source (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>), _Operate_ supplies the keystream as Form C `kl.exec` instructions of
whole blocks would; the unused bytes of the last block are discarded.

 ** In States _Ready_ and _Operate_, the Form B
    `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `Xs` sets the counter:
    `ctr` {leftarrow} `bin(Xs, j)`. It is an operation, not a State
    (Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>>).

NOTE: Setting the counter gives random access into a keystream, for example to resume an interrupted stream.
No check detects `ctr` wrapping around (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).

Code examples are given in <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-keystream-modes>> (for `b` = 128).

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-XEX-XTS-modes]]
=== XEX/XTS Modes

XEX cite:[DBLP-conf-asiacrypt-Rogaway04] turns a block cipher into a _Tweakable Block Cipher_ (TBC) cite:[DBLP-conf-crypto-LiskovRW02], whose key and tweak together select the permutation that the cipher computes.
Its two-key form builds a TBC `Ẽ` from a block cipher `E`, with keys `K1` and `K2`, tweak `T`, and plaintext `P`:

`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `Ẽ(K1, K2, T, P) = E(K1, P xor M) xor M` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` where `{nbsp}{nbsp}` `M = E(K2, T)` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` is called a _mask_.

The single-key XEX of cite:[DBLP-conf-asiacrypt-Rogaway04], whose mask is `x {interpunct} E(K, T)`, is not architected.
The Machine computes the mask once per tweak, by the `kl.setst` that sets the tweak; each block uses the current mask and then updates it by a multiplication in GF(2^b^).
`key1` is `K1`, which encrypts the data, and `key2` is `K2`, which encrypts the tweak.

XTS cite:[DBLP-journals-ieeesp-Martin10], designed for storage confidentiality and a NIST Recommendation since 2010, adds two things to XEX: the tweak identifies the data unit, and a data unit whose length is not a multiple of `b` is completed by ciphertext stealing.
Software obtains both from the XEX CC (<<KLE-XTS-from-XEX>>).
The variants differ as follows:

* `AES128_XEX` and `AES256_XEX` follow cite:[nist-SP-800-38E] and cite:[IEEE-1619-2018]:
** `update_mask` takes `V[i]` as the coefficient of `x^i^`;
** the tweak, the `INPUT` of the `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_, is `bin(i, b)` (<<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>), the data unit sequence number `i` as a `b`-bit little-endian value;
** a data unit has at most 2^20^ blocks (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
* `SM4_XEX` follows cite:[GBT-17964-2021]:
** `update_mask` takes the bits of each byte in the reverse order;
** the tweak is the 128-bit tweak of cite:[GBT-17964-2021], its first byte in byte 0.

(((KLE, Machine, XEX)))
The Machine cannot check that the two keys are independent: the provisioner must supply two independently generated keys,
a System Key Identifier must resolve to two independently generated keys in the SKS, and software that derives `key1 {vvert} key2`
(<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>) must take it from a source whose two halves are independent, such as a keystream.

Parameters::

* `b` = 128 = block size.
* `k` = key size (8|`k`).
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)     | Field
|  ii   | `k` or 64       | `key1` or System Key Identifier
| iii   | `k` or 0        | `key2`, or empty if _KeyType_ = 1 (a single SKID retrieves both keys, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>)
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key1`: `k` bits.
 ** `key2`: `k` bits. `key2` = `key1` is a configuration error (Rule <<KLE-MGR-config-data-error>>).
    It is checked whenever the keys are written: by a provisioning, by an import, and by a `kl.derive` into `key1 {vvert} key2`.
    A provisioning checks them whether the PI gives them by value or by a System Key Identifier, or the provisioning generates them (_KeyType_ = 2).
 ** `mask`: `b` bits.
 ** `StealSkip` and `StealBack`: bits 0 and 1 of _StateExtension_ respectively, which record the two steps of ciphertext stealing in _Decrypt_ (<<KLE-XTS-from-XEX>>).
    At most one of them is set, and only in _Decrypt_; the Machine supports no other _StateExtension_ @ _State_.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  i  | `k` or 64     | `key1` or System Key Identifier
| ii  | `k` or 0      | `key2`, or empty if _KeyType_ = 1 (a single SKID retrieves both keys, Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>)
| iii  | `b`           | `mask`
|===
--

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `enc_blk(K, p) -> c` encrypts block `p` using the key `K`, and returns the result `c`.
* `dec_blk(K, c) -> p` decrypts block `c` using the key `K`, and returns the result `p`.
* `update_mask(mask) -> mask` updates the mask by multiplication with a generator
of GF(2^128^): multiplication by `x` modulo `x^128^ + x^7^ + x^2^ + x + 1`.
For `AES128_XEX` and `AES256_XEX` (cite:[IEEE-1619-2018] {sect}5.2),
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `update_mask(V)` = `V << 1` `{fournbsp}` if `V[127]` = `0`
+
and
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `update_mask(V)` = `(V << 1) xor (zeros(120) @ 0b10000111)`
`{nbsp}{nbsp}` otherwise.
+
For `SM4_XEX`, with
`B~j~` = `V[8j+7:8j]`, byte `j` of `V`, `update_mask(V)` = `B'~15~ @` {ellipsis} `@ B'~0~`, where
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `B'~j~` = `B~j-1~[0] @ B~j~[7:1]` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` for `0 < j < 16`,
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `B'~0~` = `B~0~ >> 1` `{fournbsp}` if `B~15~[0]` = `0`, `{nbsp}{nbsp}` and
`B'~0~` = `(B~0~ >> 1) xor 0xE1` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` otherwise.
* `update_mask_inv(mask) -> mask` inverts `update_mask`, dividing by `x`: `update_mask_inv(update_mask(V))` = `V`.
For `AES128_XEX` and `AES256_XEX`,
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `update_mask_inv(V)` = `V >> 1` `{fournbsp}` if `V[0]` = `0`
+
and
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `update_mask_inv(V)` = `\((V xor (zeros(120) @ 0b10000111)) >> 1) xor (0b1 @ zeros(127))`
`{nbsp}{nbsp}` otherwise.
+
For `SM4_XEX`, with `B~j~` byte `j` of `V` as above, `c` = `B~0~[7]`, and `A` = `B~0~` if `c` = `0` and
`A` = `B~0~ xor 0xE1` otherwise, `update_mask_inv(V)` = `B'~15~ @` {ellipsis} `@ B'~0~`, where
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `B'~j~` = `B~j~[6:0] @ B~j+1~[7]` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` for `0 < j < 15`,
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `B'~0~` = `A[6:0] @ B~1~[7]` `{nbsp}{nbsp}` and `{nbsp}{nbsp}` `B'~15~` = `B~15~[6:0] @ c`.

State Machine::

* *States:* _Ready_, _Encrypt_, and _Decrypt_ (defined as before, cf. <<KLE-ECB-mode>>), and _Success_.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_, or _Encrypt_ -> _Encrypt_, if encryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Ready_, or _Decrypt_ -> _Decrypt_, if decryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
// ** _Decrypt_ -> _Success_, by the last `kl.exec` of ciphertext stealing.
 ** _Encrypt_, _Decrypt_ or _Success_ -> _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** Every transition, same-State `kl.setst` included, clears `StealSkip` and `StealBack`.
 ** When entering _Ready_, `mask` {leftarrow} 0.
 ** When entering _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_, the Form C ``kl.setst`` takes the tweak as `INPUT`
    and computes the initial mask:
  . `mask <- INPUT`, with `INPUT` truncated to its `b` least significant bits
    (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>);
  . `mask <- enc_blk(key2, mask)`.
 ** The Machine allows a same-State `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>), which, like any other `kl.setst` into these States, takes a new tweak and performs these two steps.

 ** In States _Encrypt_ and _Decrypt_, with `StealSkip` and `StealBack` clear, (multi-block) Form A ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected.
    For each block, they read the `INPUT` and determine the `OUTPUT` as follows:
+
--
  . In State _Encrypt_, `OUTPUT <- mask xor enc_blk(key1, INPUT xor mask)`;
    in State _Decrypt_, `OUTPUT <- mask xor dec_blk(key1, INPUT xor mask)`.
  . `mask <- update_mask(mask)`.
--
 ** In State _Decrypt_, the Form A `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_xex_state_steal` (66) starts ciphertext stealing.
    It is an operation (Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>>).
    If `StealSkip` or `StealBack` is set, it transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_; otherwise it:
  . `mask <- update_mask(mask)`;
  . sets `StealSkip`.
 ** In State _Decrypt_ with `StealSkip` set, a Form A `kl.exec` processes a single block, and its only interruption point
    is 0 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>):
  . `OUTPUT <- mask xor dec_blk(key1, INPUT xor mask)`;
  . `mask <- update_mask_inv(mask)`;
  . it clears `StealSkip` and sets `StealBack`.
 ** In State _Decrypt_ with `StealBack` set, a Form A `kl.exec` processes a single block, and its only interruption point
    is 0 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>):
  . `OUTPUT <- mask xor dec_blk(key1, INPUT xor mask)`;
  . the locker transitions to _Success_.
//

Code examples, with support for ciphertext stealing, are given in <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-basic-IV-mode>> and <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-XEX-decryption>>.

[[KLE-XTS-from-XEX,XTS ciphertext stealing]]
*Ciphertext stealing.* A data unit of whole blocks is processed, in both directions, by the plain XEX sequence over its blocks.
Otherwise, let it be `m` {ge} 1 full blocks `P~0~` {ellipsis} `P~m-1~` followed by a partial block `P~m~` of `s` bits,
with `0` < `s` < `b` and `s` a multiple of 8.
The locker is at _mask index_ `j` when its `mask` is `update_mask` applied `j` times to `enc_blk(key2, T)`, `T` being the tweak.
Blocks are processed in order (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>), so the `j`-th block after the tweak is set, counting from 0, uses mask index `j`.
`CP @ P~m~` and `CP @ C~m~` place the partial block in the least significant bits, where the first `s`/8 bytes of a string are (<<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>).

Encryption consumes the mask indices in order:

  . Issue `kl.exec` for `P~0~` {ellipsis} `P~m-2~`, obtaining `C~0~` {ellipsis} `C~m-2~` (no instruction if `m` = 1).
  . Issue `kl.exec` for `P~m-1~`, obtaining `CC` at mask index `m-1`; `C~m~` {leftarrow} `CC[s-1:0]` and `CP` {leftarrow} `CC[b-1:s]`.
  . Issue `kl.exec` for `CP @ P~m~`, obtaining `C~m-1~` at mask index `m`.
  . The ciphertext is `C~0~` {ellipsis} `C~m-1~`, `C~m~`, with `C~m~` the final `s` bits.

Decryption consumes mask index `m` before mask index `m-1`; the stealing steps of the Machine reorder the two:

  . Issue `kl.exec` for `C~0~` {ellipsis} `C~m-2~`, obtaining `P~0~` {ellipsis} `P~m-2~` (no instruction if `m` = 1); the `mask` is at index `m-1`.
  . Issue the Form A `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_xex_state_steal`, which skips mask index `m-1`.
  . Issue `kl.exec` for `C~m-1~`, obtaining `PP` at mask index `m`; the `mask` returns to index `m-1`.
  . `P~m~` {leftarrow} `PP[s-1:0]` and `CP` {leftarrow} `PP[b-1:s]`.
  . Issue `kl.exec` for `CP @ C~m~`, obtaining `P~m-1~` at mask index `m-1`; the locker transitions to _Success_.

Decryption with stealing thus adds the `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_xex_state_steal` to the data unit.
The data unit ends in _Success_, so a `kl.setst` into _Ready_ precedes the `kl.setst` that sets the next tweak.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-tweakable]]
=== Generic Tweakable Block Ciphers

The specification of a generic tweakable block cipher resembles that of a block cipher in ECB mode.
Since `kl.exec` takes a single input, a separate instruction sets the tweak.
That instruction truncates its input to the `t` bits of the tweak (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>), so a tweak shorter than the block needs no change of vector length.
A tweak longer than the block may need the vector length reconfigured between setting the tweak and processing blocks.

Some commonly used TBCs are `Deoxys-BC` cite:[Deoxys-141], `SKINNY` cite:[DBLP-conf-crypto-BeierleJKL0PSSS16], `QARMA` cite:[DBLP-journals-tosc-Avanzi17], and `QARMAv2` cite:[DBLP-journals-tosc-AvanziBDEGNR23].

Parameters::

* `b` = block size (8|`b`), with `b` {ge} 64.
* `k` = key size (8|`k`).
* `t` = tweak size (8|`t`).
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits.
 ** `tweak`: `t` bits.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|   i  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
|  ii  | `t`           | `tweak`
|===
--

State Machine::

* *States:* _Ready_, _Encrypt_, and _Decrypt_ (cf. <<KLE-ECB-mode>>).
Setting the tweak is an operation, not a State (Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>>).

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Encrypt_, if encryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Ready_, _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Decrypt_, if decryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ -> _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** When entering _Ready_, the `tweak` field is cleared.
 ** The `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ is of Form A.
    The Machine allows a same-State `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>), which leaves `tweak` unchanged.
 ** In States _Ready_, _Encrypt_ and _Decrypt_, the Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and
    auxiliary operand `INPUT` is the set-tweak operation.
    It performs `tweak <- INPUT`, with `INPUT` truncated to its `t` least significant bits (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
 ** In _Encrypt_ and _Decrypt_, (multi-block) Form A `kl.exec` instructions are expected.
    For each block, they encrypt, resp. decrypt, `INPUT` into `OUTPUT` under `key` and `tweak`.
    Every block uses the current `tweak`; the Machine never updates it by itself.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-process-VLI]]
=== Macro: Processing Variable Length Inputs into a Fixed Length Output

Many Machines process variable-length inputs into fixed-length outputs, for instance to hash, or to derive an internal fixed-length IV from a variable-length external one. They do so in specific States, such as _Hash_Absorb_.
This section describes the semantics of such a State, called _Current_State_ here.
`PROCESS_VLI` is a macro, not a subroutine: an invocation splices the behavior below into the State Machine of the
calling Machine, with each parameter replaced by the variable or function that it names, and with any change to its steps
that the invoking Machine states, such as the final piece of <<KLE-SHA-2>> and the postponed step 3.g of <<KLE-BLAKE2>>.
The macro defines how the `kl.exec` instructions of _Current_State_ and `last_block_state` consume their `INPUT`.
A `kl.setst` passes the macro no data. The `kl.setst` that enters _Current_State_ may set `max_len` (Form B), and the one that enters `last_block_state` may set `last_block_len`. Any other operand of a `kl.setst` is defined by the invoking Machine.

A Machine invokes it as:

```
PROCESS_VLI(max_len,
            block,           b,               state,           n,
            input_base,      block_base,      state_offset,    cumul_len,
            process_block(), finalize(),      mode,            granularity,
            last_block_state, process_last_block(),            last_block_len)
```
where each parameter is a _reference_ to a variable or function of the calling Machine, or `None` where the calling Machine does not define it:

. `max_len` is the total length of the data to be absorbed, *in bits*. If neither software nor the Machine sets it (see *State Machine Behavior* below), `max_len` is zero.
. `block` contains the current block being filled internally before processing. Its size is `b` bits.
. `state` is the output buffer. It may be the same as `block`. Its size is `n` bits.
. `input_base` is the offset, within the `INPUT` of the `kl.exec`, of the next data to absorb.
. `block_base` is the offset, within the current block, of the next data to absorb: within `block` if `mode` is
   `assign`, and within `state` from `state_offset` if `mode` is `xor_accumulate` (steps 3.b and 3.c).
   Each time it reaches `b`, the block is processed (step 3.g).
. `state_offset` is the offset within `state` at which the input is XORed, for instance in sponge constructions.
. `cumul_len` is the cumulative length of the data absorbed so far by the macro, or `None` if not tracked.
. `mode` is either `assign` or `xor_accumulate`, and selects how incoming data is combined with
   the destination, as specified in steps 3.b and 3.c below. It is *not* inferred from whether `block` and
   `state` denote the same variable.
. `last_block_state` is the State in which a final piece of the input is absorbed, or `None`.
. `last_block_len` is the variable of the caller Machine that contains the bit length of the final
   piece still to be absorbed, or `None` if `last_block_state` is `None`.

[IMPORTANT]
====
`input_base`, `block_base`, `cumul_len` and `last_block_len`, the last two whenever they are
given, shall denote distinct storage locations.
They are updated independently: `input_base` is set to 8 {interpunct} `klstart` at the start of every `kl.exec` (step 2), whereas
`block_base` and `cumul_len` persist across instructions.
An invocation that binds two of them to the same location is malformed: the macro would update
that location several times per iteration.

When `mode` is `xor_accumulate`, the caller shall satisfy `state_offset` + `b` {le} `n`. The
destination of step 3.c ends at bit `block_base` + `amount` + `state_offset` − 1, and
`block_base` + `amount` can reach `b`, so a larger `state_offset` would write past the end of `state`.

`mode` is an explicit parameter because the two combining rules are not interchangeable.
Sponge constructions require `xor_accumulate`: they XOR the input directly into the rate portion
of the state. A field that is *loaded* rather than absorbed, in particular key and key-like
material, requires `assign`. Selecting `xor_accumulate` where `assign` is meant combines the
incoming value with whatever the field already held. For a key field, this silently yields a key
that neither party chose.
====

The macro does not initialize the persistent state variables `block_base` and `cumul_len`, so that several invocations of it can concatenate their results.
The invoking Machine zeroes them where needed.
A Machine that enters a State with `mode` = `assign`, and whose destination field must hold a
defined value once loading completes, shall zero that field on entry to the State.

*Units.* `max_len`, `b`, `n`, `input_base`, `block_base`, `state_offset`, `cumul_len`, `last_block_len`, and `amount` are
all counted in bits, to support values of `max_len` that are not multiples of 8 bits.
However, the macro only supports values of `b` and `n` that are multiples of 8 bits,
and all `kl.exec` instructions transfer whole bytes and never a fraction of a byte.
As a consequence, `input_base` and `block_base` are multiples of 8, with two exceptions: after the final transfer of an
input whose `max_len` is not a multiple of 8, which ends the instruction (step 3.h), and after a final piece absorbed in
`last_block_state`.

Finally, three parameters are functions of the calling Machine:

* `process_block()=process_block(block, state)` processes the current block into `state`.
  It may be given as `None`, in which case no such call is made, and the macro uses `state` only in step 3.c.
* `finalize()` finalizes the processing of the input data, completing the current block where the Machine's rules require it. It may also be given as `None`, in which case no such call is made.
* `process_last_block()=process_last_block(block, state)` processes the block that holds the final bit of
  a piece absorbed in `last_block_state`, in place of `process_block()`. It may be given as `None`, in
  which case that block is processed as any other.

The last three parameters may be omitted, and are then `None`: the steps for `last_block_state` below do
not apply.

Passing `None` for both `process_block()` and `finalize()` reduces the macro to its accounting of partial progress across
`kl.exec` instructions, which is what a Machine needs when it merely has to fill a long
field from memory and has no per-block processing to perform.

A Machine whose final piece needs a State of its own passes `last_block_state`.
It also passes `process_last_block()` if the block that holds the final bit needs processing of its own.

The optional parameter `granularity` specifies the transfer unit for inputs.

* Where `max_len` = 0, `granularity` constrains no `KLLEN`: every `KLLEN` that is a multiple of 8 is absorbed, and `granularity` only restricts the interruption points (step 3.i).
* Where `max_len` != 0, a transfer violates the granularity of the State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>) if its `KLLEN` is not a whole multiple of `granularity` and `cumul_len` + `KLLEN` < `max_len`. In this test, `cumul_len` is its value when the instruction is issued, whether the instruction starts or resumes.
* Every other `KLLEN` meets the granularity of the State, so `max_len` need not be a multiple of `granularity`.
* Where `last_block_state` is `None`, the data beyond `max_len` is ignored (step 3.h, <<KLE-truncation-vs-length>>); otherwise step 1 requires each transfer to end before `max_len`. For example, with `granularity` = 128, a 96-bit IV is supplied in a single transfer, either as a 96-bit vector or as a 128-bit vector with excess bits truncated.
* Since `KLLEN` counts the bytes that a non-zero `klstart` skips, a resumed transfer with a `KLLEN` that is not a whole multiple of `granularity` can also be admitted and end before `max_len` with a partial block.

//

*Internal State Variables*::
Besides the variables that the parameters name, the Machine may define variables for `process_block()`, `process_last_block()` and `finalize()`.
`max_len` keeps its value for the duration of the computation.

*State Machine Behavior*::

** When entering State _Current_State_:
 . If software sets `max_len`, it passes it to a Form B `kl.setst`, which sets `max_len` to its auxiliary operand `Xs`.
 . Otherwise, `max_len` is set by the Machine, and the `kl.setst` that enters the State is of Form A, or of Form B where
   the invoking Machine defines its `Xs`, which then does not set `max_len`.

** While in State _Current_State_, only ``kl.exec`` instructions of Form B are expected, each of which processes its `INPUT` as follows.
+
--
. If `max_len` != 0, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ if either:
.. `cumul_len` {ge} `max_len`; or
.. `last_block_state` is not `None` and `cumul_len` + `KLLEN` − 8 {interpunct} `klstart` {ge} `max_len`.
. `input_base <- 8` {interpunct} `klstart`: 0 when the instruction starts, the point of progress when it resumes.
  Nothing else distinguishes a start from a resumption.
. While `(input_base < KLLEN)` do
.. If `max_len` != 0 then `amount <- min(KLLEN - input_base, b - block_base, max_len - cumul_len)`,
   else `amount <- min(KLLEN - input_base, b - block_base)`.
   `amount` is always strictly positive, so the loop terminates.
.. if `mode` = `assign` then:
 ... ``block[block_base + amount - 1 : block_base] <- INPUT[input_base + amount - 1 : input_base]``.
.. else, that is if `mode` = `xor_accumulate`:
 ...  XOR ``INPUT[input_base + amount - 1 : input_base]`` to ``state[block_base + amount + state_offset - 1 : block_base + state_offset]``.
.. `input_base <- input_base + amount`.
.. `block_base <- block_base + amount`.
.. If `cumul_len` is not `None`, then `cumul_len <- cumul_len + amount`.
.. If (`block_base` = `b`) then:
 ... `process_block(block, state)`, if `process_block()` is not `None`.
 ... `block_base <- 0`.
.. If `max_len` != 0 and `cumul_len = max_len` then:
 ... `finalize()`, if `finalize()` is not `None`.
 ... The instruction is terminated, even if `input_base < KLLEN`.
.. *Here*, and only here, the instruction may be interrupted while `input_base < KLLEN`, if the invocation gives no `granularity` or `input_base` is a whole multiple of it, with `klstart <- input_base / 8`.
--
+
Architected state alone determines the interruption points of `kl.exec` in _Current_State_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-interruption-point>>).
If the locker holds `block_base` = 0 when the instruction is issued, they are the byte offsets `k` for which 8 {times} `k` is a multiple of `granularity`, or every byte offset if the invocation gives no `granularity`.
Otherwise, the only interruption point is 0.
+
NOTE: Every pass but the last ends with a full block, so a halt leaves `block_base` = 0; the last pass may leave a partial block.
A halted instruction is thus resumed with `block_base` = 0, whatever `block_base` it started with, and the `klstart` that its halt recorded is an interruption point of the resumed instruction.
An instruction issued with a non-zero `block_base` admits only `klstart` = 0, but can still halt where step 3.i allows it, once its first iteration has completed the block.
At a halt, `input_base` is a whole number of bytes, so `klstart` never describes a position inside a byte, as is also the case on the output side of _Hash_Output_ (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).

** Where `last_block_state` is not `None`:
 . If `max_len` = 0, the invoking Machine sets `last_block_len` {leftarrow} `b` on entering _Current_State_.
   Software then issues a `kl.setst` naming either `last_block_state` or another State that the invoking Machine admits from
   _Current_State_, such as _Hash_Output_ in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>.
 . If `max_len` != 0, _Current_State_ absorbs fewer than `max_len` bits (step 1), and software enters
   `last_block_state`, which absorbs the rest.

** When entering `last_block_state` from _Current_State_:
 . If `max_len` = 0:
 .. A Form A `kl.setst` keeps `last_block_len` = `b`.
 .. A Form B `kl.setst` with auxiliary operand `Xs`:
 ... sets `last_block_len` {leftarrow} `Xs`;
 ... transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ unless `0` {le} `Xs` {le} `b` and `Xs` meets Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>;
 ... if `Xs` = 0, leaves the final piece empty: a later Form B `kl.exec` with a non-empty
     transfer window then transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (step 1 below, Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).
 . If `max_len` != 0, the `kl.setst` is of Form A and sets `last_block_len` {leftarrow} `max_len` − `cumul_len`.
 ..  If this new `last_block_len` > `b`, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_.
 . `finalize()` is not performed.

** In `last_block_state`, a single Form B `kl.exec` absorbs the `last_block_len` least significant bits of
   `INPUT` and ignores the other bits (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
   A `KLLEN` smaller than `last_block_len` violates the granularity of the State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).
   The `kl.exec` performs:
+
--
. If `last_block_len` = 0, the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).
. `input_base` {leftarrow} 0.
. While (`input_base` < `last_block_len`) do
.. `amount` {leftarrow} `min(last_block_len - input_base, b - block_base)`.
.. Steps 3.b to 3.f of the `kl.exec` in _Current_State_.
.. If `block_base` = `b` and either `input_base` < `last_block_len` or `process_last_block()` is `None`, then:
... `process_block(block, state)`, if `process_block()` is not `None`.
... `block_base` {leftarrow} 0.
. If `process_last_block()` is not `None`, then:
.. `process_last_block(block, state)`,
.. `block_base` {leftarrow} 0.
. `last_block_len` {leftarrow} 0.
--
+
The only interruption point of this `kl.exec` is 0.

** A `kl.setst` that transitions the locker from _Current_State_ or `last_block_state` to another State performs
   `finalize()` before the transition, if `finalize()` is not `None` and step 3.h has not performed it.
   This does not apply where the destination is:
 . _Ready_ or an Error State; or
 . `last_block_state`, from _Current_State_.

** The locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ on:
 . leaving `last_block_state`, with `last_block_len` != 0, for a Valid State other than _Ready_;
 . where `max_len` != 0 and `last_block_state` is not `None`, leaving _Current_State_ for a Valid State other than
   _Ready_ or `last_block_state`.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-GCM-mode]]
=== GCM Mode

The GCM mode was designed by David McGrew and John Viega cite:[DBLP-conf-indocrypt-McGrewV04] and became a NIST Recommendation in 2007 cite:[nist-SP-800-38D].
cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}5.1 defines GCM only for block ciphers whose block is 128 bits, such as AES and SM4 (<<KLE-exec-encodings>>).
(((KLE, Machine, GCM)))

Parameters::

* `k` = key size (64|`k`).
* `b` = 128 = block size in bits.
* `c` = 32 = counter size in bits.
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
+
--
** `key`: `k` bits.
** `J0`:  `128` bits. The pre-counter block of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1, a byte string (<<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>); its bytes 12 to 15, `J0`[127:96], hold the big-endian counter of the last counter block used, `start_ctr` before the first block.
** `auth_key` (derived): `128` bits. Rule <<KLE-MGR-recomputed-fields>> applies.
** `tag`: `128` bits. In _Set_Aux_Value_, the IV absorbed so far: the running GHASH value over it, or, if `len` = 96, the IV itself. Then the running GHASH value over the AD and the ciphertext; once finalized, the tag.
** `start_ctr`: an integer in the range 0 to 2^32^-1, the counter of the pre-counter block `J0` of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1; the first block uses the initial counter block `inc32(J0)`, of counter (`start_ctr` + 1) mod 2^32^.
** `last_block_len`: 0, or a multiple of 8 from 8 to 120. The bit length of the final, partial block of the plaintext or ciphertext.
** `len`, `block_base`, `cumul_len`, `input_base`: in _Set_Aux_Value_, the `max_len`, `block_base`, `cumul_len` and `input_base` of <<KLE-process-VLI>> for the IV. `input_base` is not serialized.
--
+
The variables `len`, `input_base`, `block_base` and `cumul_len` of the internal state may overlap with `J0`, or be separate
fields, as may be necessary if the implementation of macro `PROCESS_VLI` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>) works with integers of a
different length.
Where they overlap, they hold their values only until `finalize()` writes `J0`, and the zeroing of `cumul_len` at
completion (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>) leaves `J0` unchanged.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[cols="^8%,<15%,<15%,<62%",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field          | Description
|  i    | `k` or 64     | `key`          | Key value or System Key Identifier
| ii    | 128           | `J0`           | The pre-counter block of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1, a byte string; `J0`[127:96], its bytes 12 to 15, holds the big-endian counter of the last counter block used, `start_ctr` before the first block.
|  iii  | 128           | `tag`          | {empty}
|   iv  | 32            | `start_ctr`    | The counter of the pre-counter block `J0` before the first block, serialized as `bin(start_ctr,32)`.
|   v  | 16            | `last_block_len` | {empty}
|===

While the locker is in State _Set_Aux_Value_, `J0` is replaced by the following fields:

[cols="^8%,<15%,<15%,<62%",options="header"]
|===
| Pos.  | Size (Bits)     | Field       | Description
| ii.a  | 16              | `len`       | Total length of the IV, in bits.
| ii.b  | 16              | Padding     | Zero.
| ii.c  | 16              | `block_base`| Amount of data, in bits, absorbed so far into the current block.
| ii.d  | 48              | `cumul_len` | Cumulative length, in bits, of the data absorbed so far.
| ii.e  | 32              | Padding     | Zero.
|===
--

NOTE: There are no fields for the lengths of the AD and of the plaintext, as they are maintained by the calling software.

Machine-Specific Functions::
+
--
* `enc_blk(key, p) -> c` encrypts block `p` using the key, and returns the
  result `c`.

* `absorb(data)` absorbs data into the hash function by performing:
  . `tag <- tag xor data`.
  . `tag <- Galoismul(tag, auth_key)`.

* `Galoismul(a, b)` is multiplication in the GHASH field of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}6.3, in the representation of that section.
+
The field is GF(2)[x] modulo `x^128^ + x^7^ + x^2^ + x + 1`.
A 128-bit value `V` represents the element whose coefficient of
`x^8i+j^`, for `0` {le} `i` < `16` and `0` {le} `j` {le} `7`, is `V[8i + (7-j)]`.
+
In other words, the byte order runs forward (“little endian”:
the byte at offset 0 contains the coefficients of `x^0^` through `x^7^`,
the byte at offset 1 contains the coefficients of `x^8^` through `x^15^`,
and so on) while the bit order is reversed within each byte:
in the byte at offset 0, bit 0, the least significant bit, is the coefficient of `x^7^`,
bit 1 that of `x^6^`, and so on.
--

State Machine::

* *States:*
+
--
Besides _Ready_, _Success_, and _Failure_, the GCM Mode Machines make use of the following States:

 ** _Set_Aux_Value_: Absorb the IV; the `kl.exec` that completes it computes `J0`.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Process Associated Data (AD) into the tag.
 ** _Encrypt_: Encrypt the plaintext and absorb the ciphertext into the tag.
 ** _Enc_Last_Block_: Encrypt the final, fractional block of the plaintext.
 ** _Enc_Tag_Finalize_: Finalize the encryption tag, prepare to return its value.
 ** _Decrypt_: Absorb the ciphertext into the tag and decrypt it.
 ** _Dec_Last_Block_: Decrypt the final, fractional block of the ciphertext.
 ** _Dec_Tag_Finalize_: Finalize the computation of the tag and keep it internally.
 ** _Hash_Verify_: Compare the tag against a user supplied value.

The Associated Data (AD) and the plaintext or ciphertext are absorbed into `GHASH`
differently, which places two obligations on the
caller:

* *For the AD*, the caller must zero every bit of the final block beyond the actual end
  of the AD. `GHASH` absorbs that block whole, following cite:[nist-SP-800-38D].
  No State processes a fractional last AD block separately.

* *For the plaintext and ciphertext*, the caller must not pass a final block shorter than `b`, zero-padded, to
  _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_: the padding would be encrypted, emitting keystream, and absorbed, producing a tag that does
  not conform to cite:[nist-SP-800-38D].
  Such a block goes through State _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_.
--

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Hash_Absorb_; the `kl.exec` that completes the IV makes the second transition (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>).
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_, which restarts the IV (see below).
 ** _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Encrypt_, if encryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Decrypt_, if decryption is allowed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).
 ** _Encrypt_ -> { _Enc_Last_Block_ -> } _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Success_.
 ** _Decrypt_ -> { _Dec_Last_Block_ -> } _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Hash_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** Gated States (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>): _MachinePolicy_[0] gates _Encrypt_, _Enc_Last_Block_ and _Enc_Tag_Finalize_; _MachinePolicy_[1] gates _Decrypt_, _Dec_Last_Block_, _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ and _Hash_Verify_.

* *Behavior:*
 ** When entering State _Ready_:
  . `auth_key <- enc_blk(key, zeros(b))`;
  . `tag <- zeros(b)`.

 ** When entering State _Set_Aux_Value_:
  . A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `Xs` sets the IV length in bits: `len <- Xs`.
  . `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `8` {le} `Xs` {le} `8192`; any other `Xs`, `0` included, transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
