  . `tag`, `input_base`, `block_base` and `cumul_len` are set to zero.
  . A same-State Form B `kl.setst` repeats these operations, restarting the IV and discarding the part absorbed so far
    (Rules <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>> and <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>).

 ** While in State _Set_Aux_Value_, the IV is loaded under Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>, with _W_ = `cumul_len`, in bits, and absorbed as it is
    loaded by the macro `PROCESS_VLI` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>), called as +
  `PROCESS_VLI(len, block=tag, b, state=tag, b, input_base, block_base, 0, cumul_len, process_block(), finalize(), mode=xor_accumulate, granularity=b)`, where:
   *** `process_block()` performs `tag <- Galoismul(tag, auth_key)`; a 96-bit IV never fills a block, so it is never multiplied;
   *** `finalize()` is defined as follows:
    . If `len = 96`, then:
      .. `J0 <- bswap(bin(1,32)) @ tag[95:0]`.
+
The value of `J0` is that of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1 for a 96-bit IV, the byte string `IV {vvert} bswap(bin(1,32))`: the IV occupies bytes 0 to 11, i.e.
`J0[95:0]`, and the counter occupies bytes 12 to 15 as a *big-endian* integer.

    . Otherwise, let `s <- 128 {interpunct} ceil(len/128) - len`, and the computation of `J0 := GHASH(IV {vvert} 0^s+64^ {vvert} bswap(bin(len,64)), auth_key)` is completed by performing:
      .. if `block_base != 0`, then `tag <- Galoismul(tag, auth_key)`.
      .. `tag <- tag xor (bswap(bin(len,64)) @ zeros(64))`.
      .. `tag <- Galoismul(tag, auth_key)`.
      .. `J0 <- tag`.
    . `start_ctr <- int(bswap(J0[127:96]))`.
    . `tag <- zeros(b)`.
    . Transitions to State _Hash_Absorb_.

 ** When leaving State _Set_Aux_Value_:
  . the `kl.exec` that completes the IV performs `finalize()` (step 3.h of <<KLE-process-VLI>>), which transitions the
    locker to _Hash_Absorb_;
  . a `kl.setst` naming _Ready_ performs no `finalize()`: the partial IV is discarded, and `tag`, `J0`, `len`,
    `block_base` and `cumul_len` are zeroed (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>);
  . a `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Absorb_ transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_: entering _Hash_Absorb_
    requires a complete IV (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>).

[NOTE]
====
The IV is absorbed into `tag`, not into `J0`, and only the finished value is copied to `J0`.
`tag` is free for this: entering _Set_Aux_Value_ zeroes it, which is GHASH's initial value.
The slot of `J0` in the Serialized Content instead carries `len`, `block_base` and `cumul_len` while this State is
active. Accumulating into `J0` would place the running hash and those counters in the same 128 bits, so a CC
exported part-way through a non-96-bit IV could not resume.
====

 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_, (multi-block) Form B `kl.exec` instructions absorb each data block into `tag`:
    `absorb(INPUT)`.
 ** The `kl.setst` from _Hash_Absorb_ into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_ is of Form A.
** In State _Encrypt_, (multi-block) Form A `kl.exec` instructions encrypt `INPUT` into
   `OUTPUT` and absorb `OUTPUT` into `tag`:
+
--
  * `local ctr : int` - the running counter, as an integer.
  * `local tmp : bits(b)`

[]
  . `ctr <- int(bswap(J0[127:96]))`   // counter as an integer
  . `ctr <- (ctr + 1) mod 2^32^`.
  . If `ctr` = `(start_ctr - 1) mod 2^32^`, then: (at most 2^32^ − 2 blocks)
   .. the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_.
   . else:
  .. `J0[127:96] <- bswap(bin(ctr,32))`.  // counter back to big-endian
  .. `tmp <- INPUT xor enc_blk(key, J0)`.
  .. `absorb(tmp)`.
  .. `OUTPUT <- tmp`.
--
** When entering State _Enc_Last_Block_:
 . A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_enc_last_block` and auxiliary operand `Xs` sets the bit length of the last block:
   `last_block_len <- Xs`.
 . `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `8` {le} `Xs` {le} `120` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>); any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
+
NOTE: cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] admits any bit length, but numbers the bits of a byte from the most significant.
A truncation to the least significant bits does not map onto that order; hence the multiples of 8.

** In State _Enc_Last_Block_, a single Form A `kl.exec` encrypts its `INPUT` into its `OUTPUT` and
   absorbs `OUTPUT` into `tag`:
+
--
  * `local ctr : int` - the running counter, as an integer.
  * `local tmp : bits(b)`

[]
  . If `last_block_len = 0`, the final block has already been processed: the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).
  . `ctr <- int(bswap(J0[127:96]))`.
  . `ctr <- (ctr + 1) mod 2^32^`.
  . If `ctr` = `(start_ctr - 1) mod 2^32^`, then:
   .. the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_.
  . else:
   .. `J0[127:96] <- bswap(bin(ctr,32))`.
   .. `tmp <- (zeros(128-last_block_len) @ INPUT[last_block_len-1:0])` +
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}xor (zeros(128-last_block_len) @ enc_blk(key, J0)[last_block_len-1:0])`.
   .. `absorb(tmp)`.
   .. `OUTPUT <- tmp`.
   .. `last_block_len <- 0`
--

** If a `kl.setst` leaves _Enc_Last_Block_ or _Dec_Last_Block_ before the `kl.exec` of that State, no final block is processed: `tag` and `J0` are unchanged, and the message ends with the blocks already processed.

** When entering State _Enc_Tag_Finalize_:
 . A Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_enc_tag_finalize` and auxiliary operand `INPUT` supplies the length block, which software computes:
 .. `INPUT <- bswap(bin(len_in_bits(plaintext), 64)) @ bswap(bin(len_in_bits(AD), 64))`.
 .. `len_in_bits(plaintext)` counts only the plaintext that _Encrypt_ and _Enc_Last_Block_ processed; a final block that a `kl.setst` omitted is not counted.
. `INPUT` is truncated to its 128 least significant bits (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
. The tag is computed and retained internally:
 .. `absorb(INPUT)`, then
 .. `tag <- tag xor enc_blk(key, bswap(bin(start_ctr,32)) @ J0[95:0])`.

** In State _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, a single Form C `kl.exec` writes `tag` to `OUTPUT`, and the locker
   transitions to _Success_.
.  A `KLLEN` smaller than 128 must be 120, 112, 104, 96 or 64 (cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}5.2.1.2), and `OUTPUT` then
   receives `tag[KLLEN-1:0]`, the truncated tag `MSB~KLLEN~(T)` of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1.
. Any other `KLLEN` below 128 violates the granularity of the State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).

** In State _Decrypt_, (multi-block) Form A `kl.exec` instructions absorb the ciphertext
   `INPUT` into `tag`, then decrypt `INPUT` into `OUTPUT`:
+
--
 * `local ctr : int` - the running counter, as an integer.

[]
 . `ctr <- int(bswap(J0[127:96]))`.
 . `ctr <- (ctr + 1) mod 2^32^`.
 . If `ctr` = `(start_ctr - 1) mod 2^32^`, then:
  .. the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_.
 . else:
  .. `absorb(INPUT)`.
  .. `J0[127:96] <- bswap(bin(ctr,32))`.
  .. `OUTPUT <- INPUT xor enc_blk(key, J0)`.
--

** When entering State _Dec_Last_Block_:
 . A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_dec_last_block` and auxiliary operand `Xs` sets the bit length of the last block:
   `last_block_len <- Xs`.
 . `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `8` {le} `Xs` {le} `120` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>); any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

** In State _Dec_Last_Block_, a single Form A `kl.exec` absorbs the zero-padded final block
   of the ciphertext into `tag` and decrypts `INPUT` into `OUTPUT`:
+
--
  * `local ctr : int` - the running counter, as an integer.
  * `local tmp : bits(b)`

[]
  . If `last_block_len = 0`, the final block has already been processed: the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).
  . `ctr <- int(bswap(J0[127:96]))`.
  . `ctr <- (ctr + 1) mod 2^32^`.
  . If `ctr` = `(start_ctr - 1) mod 2^32^`, then:
   .. the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_.
  . else:
  .. `tmp <- zeros(128-last_block_len) @ INPUT[last_block_len-1:0]`.
  .. `absorb(tmp)`.
  .. `J0[127:96] <- bswap(bin(ctr,32))`.
  .. `OUTPUT <- tmp xor (zeros(128-last_block_len) @ enc_blk(key, J0)[last_block_len-1:0])`.
  .. `last_block_len <- 0`
--

** When entering State _Dec_Tag_Finalize_:
 . A Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_dec_tag_finalize` and auxiliary operand `INPUT` supplies the length block, which software computes:
 .. `INPUT <- bswap(bin(len_in_bits(ciphertext), 64)) @ bswap(bin(len_in_bits(AD), 64))`.
 .. `len_in_bits(ciphertext)` counts only the ciphertext that _Decrypt_ and _Dec_Last_Block_ processed; a final block that a `kl.setst` omitted is not counted.
. `INPUT` is truncated to its 128 least significant bits (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
. The tag is computed:
.. `absorb(INPUT)`, then
.. `tag <- tag xor enc_blk(key, bswap(bin(start_ctr,32)) @ J0[95:0])`.

** When entering State _Hash_Verify_, a Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_verify` and auxiliary operand `INPUT` supplies the value
   against which the internally computed tag is compared (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>):
+
--
 . The tag length is `t` = `min(KLLEN, 128)`, which must be one of 128, 120, 112, 104, 96 or 64
   (cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}5.2.1.2).
 . Any other `t` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_, and no comparison is made.
 . `INPUT[t-1:0]` is compared with `tag[t-1:0]`. If they match, the locker transitions to _Success_; otherwise, to
   _Failure_.
--
+
NOTE: Truncated tags are prefixes of one another.
The floor of 64 bits excludes the 32 bits that cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] admits for some applications.
With it, finding the tag through a verification oracle takes about 2^64^ trials, even though each verification chooses its own `t`. The caller must use one `t` per key (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
+
NOTE: _Decrypt_ and _Dec_Last_Block_ release the plaintext block by block, before _Hash_Verify_ checks the tag,
whereas cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] returns no plaintext when the check fails. The caller must not use the plaintext
before the locker reaches _Success_, and must discard it on _Failure_.
The same holds for the other AEAD Machines: <<KLE-GCM-with-IV-mode>>, <<KLE-GCM-SIV-mode>> (whose
_Dec_Tag_Finalize_ verifies the tag), <<KLE-OCB-mode>>, <<KLE-OCB-with-nonce-mode>>, <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128>>,
<<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-wsn>> and <<KLE-Ascon-AEAD128-N-masking>>.

//

<<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-GCM-encryption>> and <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-GCM-decryption>> provide
code examples for GCM encryption and decryption.

//

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-GCM-with-IV-mode]]
=== GCM with Set IV

The mode of operation “GCM with a Set IV” is a variant of GCM in which the initialization vector (IV) is provisioned with the key, rather than supplied by software for each message.

This Machine is the same as GCM (<<KLE-GCM-mode>>), except that it does not set the IV.
A 96-bit IV is carried in the Provisioning Input, and provisioning forms `J0` from it as cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1
does for a 96-bit IV; `J0` itself is never provisioned.
State _Set_Aux_Value_ and everything that GCM specifies for it therefore do not apply, and _Hash_Absorb_ is entered
from _Ready_. The differences follow.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[cols="^8%,<15%,<15%,<62%",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field       | Description
|  ii   | `k` or 64     | `key`       | Key value or System Key Identifier
| iii   | 96            | `IV`        | The IV of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1. Only a 96-bit IV is supported.
|===
--

* *Internal State*: {empty}
+
--
Same as for GCM.

Upon provisioning, before the operations of entering _Ready_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-management-no-state-operations>>),
`J0 <- bswap(bin(1,32)) @ IV`, the byte string `IV {vvert} bswap(bin(1,32))` of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}7.1, and `start_ctr <- 1`. `start_ctr` is 1 in every
CC of this Machine: an import whose Serialized Content holds another value completes into Error State _Invalid_
(Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
--

* *Serialized Content*: {empty}
+
--
Same as for GCM.
--

State Machine::

* *States and Allowed Transitions:* {empty}
+
--
Same as for GCM, with the following exceptions:

** There is no State _Set_Aux_Value_. The transition is from _Ready_ to _Hash_Absorb_.
--

* *State Machine Behavior:* {empty}
+
--
The differences with respect to GCM are:

 ** The ``kl.setst`` into State _Hash_Absorb_ must be of Form A, i.e., _without any additional inputs_: the IV is already in the PI.
 ** Entering State _Ready_ also restores the provisioned `J0`, by `J0[127:96] <- bswap(bin(1,32))`: a later message repeats the provisioned GCM invocation. Software that needs another IV provisions another locker.
--

This Machine is not a `kl.derive` destination (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>): its `key` and IV are configured together, by provisioning or import.

[NOTE]
====
This Machine protects the confidentiality of the IV, not against its reuse (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>): encrypting a different message under the same `key` and `J0` violates the uniqueness requirement of cite:[nist-SP-800-38D] {sect}8.

A key that software knows, derived into `key` while `J0` is kept, would let it recover `J0` from the keystream.
So would the same `key` in another locker that software can use, if that locker:

* computes the inverse block cipher under the key, such as a decrypting locker of <<KLE-ECB-mode>>: with it, software recovers the IV, and so `J0`, from a single keystream block;
* computes the block cipher under the key, such as an encrypting locker of <<KLE-ECB-mode>> or a locker of <<KLE-keystream-modes>>: with it, software tests guesses of the IV; or
* takes an IV that software chooses, such as a locker of <<KLE-GCM-mode>> or a locker of this Machine provisioned with a guessed IV: with it, software also tests guesses of the IV.

Software holds the same key in another locker when it can provision, by its SKID, a System Key that the SKS allows for that locker's Machine.

A provisioned `J0` would let software choose the counter blocks that the Machine encrypts: with
`J0` = 0^96^ {vvert} `FFFFFFFF`, a decrypt-only locker would output `E(K, 0)`, the GHASH key, and with `J0` = `N` {vvert} 0^32^
the tag mask of IV `N`, so that software could forge tags under a key that it never sees.
====

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-GCM-SIV-mode]]
=== GCM-SIV Mode

The GCM-SIV mode is described in cite:[DBLP-journals-iacr-GueronLL17] and cite:[RFC8452].
This specification defines GCM-SIV only for AES, whose block is 128 bits, as cite:[RFC8452] does (<<KLE-exec-encodings>>); the definition will be extended to other block ciphers if needed.
(((KLE, Machine, GCM-SIV)))

The decryption path of GCM-SIV resembles that of GCM; the encryption path differs:

* *Encryption:* _Hash_Absorb_ absorbs the whole associated data (AD) and plaintext. _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ then absorbs the lengths and produces the SIV, which is also the tag. _Encrypt_ then makes a second pass over the plaintext, with the SIV as its initialization vector.
* *Decryption:* _Hash_Absorb_ absorbs only the AD. _Decrypt_ decrypts the ciphertext and absorbs the resulting plaintext. _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ then absorbs the lengths, recomputes the SIV, and compares it internally with the SIV set in _Set_Aux_Value_2_.

//

Parameters::

* `k` = key size, either 128 or 256: cite:[RFC8452] defines AES-GCM-SIV only for these two.
* `b` = 128 = block size.
* `c` = 32 = counter size.
* Granularity: `b`.
* The authentication tag is `b` = 128 bits long.
* A plaintext or ciphertext holds at most 2^32^ blocks, the final partial block included: the `P_MAX` of cite:[RFC8452] {sect}6. The caller must keep the AD within the `A_MAX` of cite:[RFC8452] {sect}6, 2^36^ bytes (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
  ** `key`: `k` bits.
 ** `enc_key` (derived): `k` bits.
 ** `auth_key` (derived): 128 bits. Rule <<KLE-MGR-recomputed-fields>> applies to both.
 ** `nonce`: 96 bits.
 ** `ctr`: the number of plaintext or ciphertext blocks encrypted or decrypted, an integer in the range 0 to 2^32^, held and serialized on 33 bits: bits [31:0] in
    field iii, bit 32 in field vi.
 ** `SIV`: 128 bits.
 ** `tmp`: 128 bits.
 ** `last_block_len`: 7 bits, an integer in the range 0 to 127. The bit length of the final, partial block of the plaintext or ciphertext.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)     | Field
|    i  |  `k` or 64      | `key` or System Key Identifier
|   ii  |  96             | `nonce`
|  iii  |  32             | `ctr[31:0]`
|   iv  |  128            | `SIV`
|    v  |  128            | `tmp`
|   vi  |  16             | `ctr[32]` in bit 15, `last_block_len` in bits [6:0], the other bits zero
|===
--

//

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `enc_blk(key, p) -> c` encrypts block `p` using the key, and returns the result `c`.
* `RFC8452_KeyDeriv(k, key, nonce)` derives the per-nonce message-encryption and
  message-authentication keys of cite:[RFC8452] {sect}4:
+
[[KLE-GCM-SIV-KeyDeriv]]
.AES-GCM-SIV Key Derivation
[]
----
function RFC8452_KeyDeriv(k : int, key : bits(k), nonce : bits(96))
    → struct{enc_key : bits(k), auth_key : bits(128)} =
{
  local A : array [0..5] of bits(128);
  local E : bits(k);
  local i : int;

  foreach(i from 0 to k/64 + 1) do:
    A[i] ← enc_blk(key, nonce @ bin(i,32));
  foreach(i from 2 to k/64 + 1) do:
    E[64*i-65 : 64*i-128] ← A[i][63:0];

  return struct { enc_key = E, auth_key = A[1][63:0] @ A[0][63:0] };
}
----
+
The loop makes `k`/64 + 2 calls, four for `k` = 128 and six for `k` = 256, and keeps the low
64 bits of each: these are the “first eight bytes” of cite:[RFC8452] under <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>.
* `absorb(data)` absorbs data into the hash function by performing a step of POLYVAL:
  . `tmp <- tmp xor data`.
  . `tmp <- Montmul(tmp, auth_key)`, with the `Montmul` of the POLYVAL of <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-SCC-POLYVAL>>.

State Machine::
  * *States:*
+
--
Besides _Ready_, _Success_, and _Failure_, the GCM-SIV Machine makes use of the following States:

 ** _Set_Aux_Value_: Set the value of `nonce`.
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_2_: Set the SIV.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Absorb the AD and, on the encryption path, the plaintext into the partial tag `tmp`.
 ** _Encrypt_: Encrypt the plaintext.
 ** _Enc_Last_Block_: Encrypt the final, fractional block of the plaintext.
 ** _Enc_Tag_Finalize_: Finalize the SIV with the AD and plaintext lengths, and return it.
 ** _Decrypt_: Decrypt the ciphertext and absorb the resulting plaintext into the partial tag.
 ** _Dec_Last_Block_: Decrypt the final, fractional block of the ciphertext.
 ** _Dec_Tag_Finalize_: Finalize the tag with the AD and plaintext lengths, then compare it against the SIV.
--

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_.
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_2_ or _Hash_Absorb_.
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_2_ -> _Hash_Absorb_.
 ** For encryption: _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Encrypt_ { -> _Enc_Last_Block_ }.
    The path ends in _Encrypt_ or _Enc_Last_Block_, not in _Success_, since the _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ `kl.exec` outputs the tag;
    the next message starts with a return to _Ready_.
 ** The `kl.exec` of _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ synthesizes `SIV` and transitions the locker to _Encrypt_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>).
 ** For decryption: _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Decrypt_ -> { _Dec_Last_Block_ -> } _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** Gated States (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>): _MachinePolicy_[0] gates _Encrypt_, _Enc_Last_Block_ and _Enc_Tag_Finalize_; _MachinePolicy_[1] gates _Decrypt_, _Dec_Last_Block_ and _Dec_Tag_Finalize_.
 ** A `kl.setst` that leaves a last-block State before its `kl.exec`:
 *** From _Dec_Last_Block_ into _Dec_Tag_Finalize_: the final block is omitted from `tmp`, and so from the recomputed SIV.
 *** From _Enc_Last_Block_: only the final ciphertext block stays unproduced, since the SIV already covers the whole plaintext.
 ** A final plaintext or ciphertext block shorter than `b` goes through _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_.

* *Behavior:*
** Upon entering State _Ready_:
  *** The following initialization operations are performed:
   . `nonce <- 0`, `ctr <- 0`, `tmp <- 0` and `SIV <- 0`.

** When entering State _Set_Aux_Value_:
   . A Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `INPUT` is expected.
   . `nonce <- INPUT[95:0]` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
   . (`enc_key`, `auth_key`) `<-` `RFC8452_KeyDeriv(k, key, nonce)`.

** When entering State _Set_Aux_Value_2_:
   . A Form C `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value_2` and auxiliary operand `INPUT` is expected.
   . `SIV <- INPUT[127:0]` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
+
[NOTE]
====
This State is meant to serve the decryption path.
Entering it during encryption is harmless: an externally set `SIV` never feeds the encryption keystream. The encryption path reaches _Encrypt_ only through the _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ `kl.exec`, which overwrites `SIV` with the synthetic value, and it reaches _Enc_Last_Block_ only from _Encrypt_.
Skipping it during decryption leaves `SIV` at the zero that entering _Ready_ set.
====

** The `kl.setst` into _Hash_Absorb_, _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, _Decrypt_ or _Dec_Tag_Finalize_ is of Form A.

** In State _Set_Aux_Value_ or _Set_Aux_Value_2_, a `kl.setst` naming that same State transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_
   (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>):
   . The `kl.setst` that enters the State sets the value.
   . The State loads no field by `kl.exec`.

** In State _Hash_Absorb_, each (multi-block) Form B `kl.exec` absorbs its data blocks into `tmp`: `absorb(INPUT)`.
   The caller zero-pads the following, each separately to a multiple of 16 bytes, as cite:[RFC8452] {sect}4 specifies:
+
--
. The AD.
. On the encryption path, the plaintext.

Every bit of the final partial block beyond the end of its data is zero.
--

** In State _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, a single Form A `kl.exec` is expected, whose `INPUT` the caller sets to
   `bin(len_in_bits(plaintext), 64) @ bin(len_in_bits(AD), 64)`; `INPUT` is truncated to its 128 least significant bits
   (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>). It finalizes the SIV:
   . `absorb(INPUT)`.
   . `tmp <- enc_blk(enc_key, 0 @ tmp[126:96] @ (tmp[95:0] xor nonce))`.
   . `SIV <- tmp` and `OUTPUT <- SIV`.
   . The state transitions to _Encrypt_.

** In State _Encrypt_, (multi-block) Form A ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected, encrypting `INPUT` into `OUTPUT`
      as follows:
+
--
  . If `ctr` = 2^32^, then the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than a message may hold.
  . ``OUTPUT <- INPUT xor enc_blk(enc_key, 1 @ SIV[126:32] @ bin\((int(SIV[31:0]) + ctr) mod 2^32^, 32))``.
  . `ctr <- ctr + 1`.
--
** When entering State _Enc_Last_Block_:
   . A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_enc_last_block` and auxiliary operand `Xs` is expected, where `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `8` {le} `Xs` {le} `120` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
   . `last_block_len <- Xs`, the bit length of the last block.
+
Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

** In State _Enc_Last_Block_, a single Form A ``kl.exec`` instruction is expected, encrypting `INPUT` into `OUTPUT`
      as follows:
+
--
. If `last_block_len = 0`, the final block has already been processed: the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).
. If `ctr` = 2^32^, then the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than a message may hold.
    . ``OUTPUT <- zeros(128-last_block_len) @ (INPUT[last_block_len-1:0] xor enc_blk(enc_key, 1 @ SIV[126:32] @ bin\((int(SIV[31:0]) + ctr) mod 2^32^, 32))[last_block_len-1:0])``.
    . `ctr <- ctr + 1`.
    . `last_block_len <- 0`.
--

** In State _Decrypt_, (multi-block) Form A ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected, decrypting `INPUT` into `OUTPUT` and then absorbing the plaintext `OUTPUT` into `tmp`:
+
--
. If `ctr` = 2^32^, then the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than a message may hold.
. ``OUTPUT <- INPUT xor enc_blk(enc_key, 1 @ SIV[126:32] @ bin\((int(SIV[31:0]) + ctr) mod 2^32^, 32))``.
. `absorb(OUTPUT)`.
. `ctr <- ctr + 1`.
--
** When entering State _Dec_Last_Block_:
   . A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_dec_last_block` and auxiliary operand `Xs` is expected, where `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `8` {le} `Xs` {le} `120` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
   . `last_block_len <- Xs`.
+
Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

** In State _Dec_Last_Block_, a single Form A ``kl.exec`` instruction is expected, decrypting `INPUT` into `OUTPUT` and
      absorbing the zero-padded plaintext into the partial tag, as follows:
+
--
. If `last_block_len = 0`, the final block has already been processed: the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).
. If `ctr` = 2^32^, then the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than a message may hold.
    . ``OUTPUT <- zeros(128-last_block_len) @ (INPUT[last_block_len-1:0] xor enc_blk(enc_key, 1 @ SIV[126:32] @ bin\((int(SIV[31:0]) + ctr) mod 2^32^, 32))[last_block_len-1:0])``.
    . `absorb(OUTPUT)`.
    . `ctr <- ctr + 1`.
    . `last_block_len <- 0`.
--


** In State _Dec_Tag_Finalize_, a single Form B `kl.exec` is expected, whose `INPUT` the caller sets to
   `bin(len_in_bits(plaintext), 64) @ bin(len_in_bits(AD), 64)`; `INPUT` is truncated to its 128 least significant bits
   (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>).
   Since the SIV was supplied first, this `kl.exec` performs the comparison itself (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>):
   . `absorb(INPUT)`.
   . `tmp <- enc_blk(enc_key, 0 @ tmp[126:96] @ (tmp[95:0] xor nonce))`.
   . If `tmp` = `SIV`, the state transitions to _Success_; otherwise, it transitions to _Failure_.

//   Repeating the operations of entering the State would admit a second final block (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).

//

Sample code is given in <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-GCM-SIV-mode-examples>>.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-OCB-mode]]
=== OCB Mode

Only OCB3 is architected, following RFC{nbsp}7253 cite:[RFC7253] and cite:[DBLP-journals-joc-KrovetzR21], and only for AES, as in the AEAD algorithms of cite:[RFC7253] (<<KLE-exec-encodings>>).
The state machine follows RFC{nbsp}7253 except in notation.
Bit positions count from 0 at the least significant bit (<<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>>); RFC{nbsp}7253 counts them from 1 at the most significant bit.
A block is a little-endian byte string: its byte 0 is the first byte of the RFC{nbsp}7253 string.
A formula over RFC{nbsp}7253 bit positions is therefore computed on the `bswap` view of the block, either with an explicit `bswap` or in a function or variable whose name ends in `_be`.
Block indices, `index` included, start from 1 as in RFC{nbsp}7253.

Parameters::
+
--
* `b` = 128 = block size.
* `k` = key size. Currently, it can only be 128, 192, or 256.
* `MAX_BLOCKS` = 2^48^ = maximum number of blocks, a final partial block included, in the AD or in the plaintext.
  This specification sets this bound: cite:[RFC7253] places no limit on the length of a message.
  The caller keeps the uniqueness of the nonce and the recommendation of
  cite:[RFC7253] {sect}5 that a key process at most 2^48^ blocks in total, the associated data included
  (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
* Granularity: `b`.
--

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64      | `key` or System Key Identifier
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
  ** `key`: `k` bits.
 ** `N`: 120 bits.
 ** `N_len`: 7 bits. Bit length of the nonce. It cannot exceed 120.
 ** `HasN`: bit 0 of _StateExtension_, set once `N` holds a loaded nonce (Rule <<KLE-MGR-load-long-field>>).
 ** `L*`, `offset`, `hash_A`, `checksum_P`: 128 bits each.
 ** `L$` and `L[i]` (derived): 128 bits each, for `0` {le} `i` {le} `floor(log_2(MAX_BLOCKS))` = 48; `L[i]` is `L~i~` of cite:[RFC7253]. Rule <<KLE-MGR-recomputed-fields>> applies to them.
 ** `tag_len`: 8 bits. Bit length of the tag. Currently, the allowed values are 64, 96, and 128.
 ** `last_block_len`: 7 bits, an integer in the range 0 to 127. The bit length of the final, partial block of the AD, the plaintext or the ciphertext; 0 for an empty final block.
 ** `index`: 49 bits, corresponding to `i` in the loops in RFC{nbsp}7253. It is 0 from the entry into _Ready_ to the entry into _Hash_Absorb_, and then ranges from 1 to `MAX_BLOCKS` + 1 = 2^48^ + 1.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits) | Field
|   i   | `k` or 64   | `key` or System Key Identifier
|   ii  | 120         | `N`
|  iii  |   7         | `N_len`, the bit length of the nonce
|   iv  |   1         | Padding
|    v  | 128         | `L*`; the derived `L$` and `L[i]` are not serialized (Rule <<KLE-MGR-recomputed-fields>>)
|   vi  | 128         | `offset` (current offset)
|  vii  | 128         | `hash_A`
| viii  | 128         | `checksum_P` (used also for the final tag)
|   ix  |  49         | `index`
|    x  |   2         | `tag_len`, encoded as `tag_len`/32 − 2. The value 3 is not produced; an import that carries it transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
|   xi  |   7         | `last_block_len`
|===
--

//

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `enc_blk(K,p) -> c` encrypts block `p` using the key `K` and returns the result `c`.
* `dec_blk(K,c) -> p` decrypts block `c` using the key `K` and returns the result `p`.
* `ntz(n)` = the number of trailing zero bits in the base-2 representation of the positive integer `n`.
Formally, `ntz(n)` is the largest integer `x` for which `2^x^` divides `n`.
* `ocb_pad(X, n)` = the `b`-bit value holding the `n`-bit partial block `X[n-1:0]`
  followed, in string order, by a single `1` bit and then zeros. This is
  the padding of a partial block of cite:[RFC7253] {sect}4.2, and under
  <<Zkl-notation.adoc#KLE-Notation>> it is
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `ocb_pad(X, n)` = `zeros(120-n) @ 0b10000000 @ X[n-1:0]`.
+
The terminating `1` is the *most significant* bit of byte `n/8`, not bit `n` of the
value: in string order it immediately follows the last data bit, and within a byte the
first bit of the string is that byte's most significant bit. `ocb_pad` is defined for
`n` a multiple of 8 with `0` {le} `n` {le} 120; for `n` = 0 it is
`zeros(120) @ 0b10000000`.
* `double(S)`: doubling in the OCB3 field.
cite:[RFC7253] specifies this operation over a big-endian string, hence:
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `double(S) <- bswap(update_mask(bswap(S)))`, +
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` with the `update_mask` of `AES128_XEX` (<<KLE-XEX-XTS-modes>>).
* `nonce_be(N, n)` = the big-endian view of the `n`-bit nonce bit string held in `N`.
  cite:[RFC7253] treats the nonce as a bit string whose first bit is the most significant, so
  when it is serialized into bytes the final, possibly partial byte is *left-aligned*: its
  `r` = `n` mod 8 most significant bits carry nonce and its remaining bits are padding. With
  `q` = {lceil}``n``/8{rceil} the number of bytes so occupied,
+
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp}` `nonce_be(N, n)` = `bswap(N[8q-1:0], q) >> ((8 - r) mod 8)`.
+
The first bit of the nonce string is `nonce_be(N, n)[n-1]` and the last is bit `0`.
For `r` = 0 this reduces to `bswap(N[n-1:0])`, so a nonce occupying a whole number of bytes is
unaffected. `bswap` alone cannot express the general case, since it is defined only over byte
strings.

//

State Machine::
* *States:*
Besides _Ready_, _Success_, and _Failure_, the Machines implementing OCB Mode make use of the following States:
+
--
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_: Set the length and the value of the nonce.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Set the tag length, and process the AD.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_: Set the bit length, possibly zero, of the final block of the AD, and process that block. Every message passes through this State.
 ** _Encrypt_: Process the plaintext by absorbing it into the tag and encrypting it.
 ** _Enc_Last_Block_: Provide the last block of the plaintext, then process it.
 ** _Enc_Tag_Finalize_: Return the tag.
 ** _Decrypt_: Process the ciphertext by decrypting it and absorbing the plaintext into the tag.
 ** _Dec_Last_Block_: Provide the last block of the ciphertext, then process it.
 ** _Hash_Verify_: Compare a user-provided value with the internally computed tag.
--

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_.
 ** _Set_Aux_Value_ -> _Set_Aux_Value_, which restarts the nonce load (see below).
 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ -> _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_.
 ** _Encrypt_ -> _Enc_Last_Block_ -> _Enc_Tag_Finalize_ -> _Success_.
 ** _Decrypt_ -> _Dec_Last_Block_ -> _Hash_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** The `kl.exec` of _Enc_Last_Block_, resp. _Dec_Last_Block_, makes the transition to _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, resp.
    _Hash_Verify_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-machine-transitions>>).
 ** Gated States (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>): _MachinePolicy_[0] gates _Encrypt_, _Enc_Last_Block_ and _Enc_Tag_Finalize_; _MachinePolicy_[1] gates _Decrypt_, _Dec_Last_Block_ and _Hash_Verify_.

* *Behavior:*
** Upon entering State _Ready_:
  *** The following initialization operations are performed:
   . `N <- zeros(120)`.
   . `N_len <- 0`.
   . `HasN` is cleared.
   . `hash_A <- zeros(128)`.
   . `checksum_P <- zeros(128)`.
   . `index <- 0`.
   . `tag_len <- 128`.

** When entering State _Set_Aux_Value_:
+
--
. A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `Xs` is expected, where `Xs` must satisfy `6` {le} `Xs` {le} `120`.
  Every bit length in that range is admissible (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>): the nonce is a bit string and need not fill whole bytes.
. `N_len <- Xs` and `N <- zeros(120)`, and `HasN` is cleared.

Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
--
[NOTE]
====
cite:[RFC7253] itself places no lower bound on the nonce length. KLE requires at least 6 bits
because a shorter nonce leaves the `bottom` and `Stretch` derivation of cite:[RFC7253] {sect}4.2
without enough entropy to remain sound, as analyzed in cite:[cryptoeprint-2023-326]. An
implementation of this specification is therefore not a general RFC{nbsp}7253 implementation for
nonces shorter than 6 bits.
====

** While in State _Set_Aux_Value_, a Form B `kl.exec` loads `N` from the `q` = {lceil}``N_len``/8{rceil}
   least significant bytes of `INPUT`, which is truncated to its `8q` least significant bits (Rule <<KLE-MGR-fixed-width-input>>):
+
--
. `N <- zeros(120 - 8q) @ INPUT[8q-1:0]`, with the `(8 - N_len) mod 8` least significant bits of byte `q-1` cleared:
they pad the final, partial byte of the nonce string, are not part of the nonce, and are ignored.
. `HasN` is set.

Each `kl.exec` in this State processes a single block, so its only interruption point is 0 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>).

A repeated `kl.exec` rewrites `N`.

A `kl.setst` naming _Set_Aux_Value_ in this State restarts the load (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-same-state>>): it performs the operations of entering the State with its new `Xs`.
--

** When entering State _Hash_Absorb_, which requires `HasN` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>):
+
--
. A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_absorb` and auxiliary operand `Xs` is expected, where `Xs` must be 64, 96 or 128.
. `index <- 1` and `tag_len <- Xs`.
. `offset <- zeros(b)` – this is `Offset~0~` in the RFC.
. `L* <- enc_blk(key, zeros(b))` – this is `L~*~` in the RFC.
. `L$ <- double(L*)`, `L[0] <- double(L$)`, and `L[i] <- double(L[i-1])` for all `i` with `0 < i` {le} 48, possibly lazily.

Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
--

** In State _Hash_Absorb_, (multi-block) `kl.exec` instructions are expected to be of Form B, absorbing a data block `INPUT` into `hash_A` as follows:
+
--
 . If `index` > `MAX_BLOCKS`, then the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than the AD may hold.
 . `offset <- offset xor L[ntz(index)]`.
 . `hash_A <- hash_A xor enc_blk(key, INPUT xor offset)`.
 . `index <- index + 1`.
--

** When entering State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_:
+
--
. A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_last_block` and auxiliary operand `Xs` is expected, where `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `0` {le} `Xs` {le} `120` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
. `last_block_len <- Xs`, the bit length of the final block of the AD.

Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
--

** In State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, a single Form B `kl.exec` absorbs the last block of the AD into `hash_A`:
+
--
  * `tmp : bits(b)`

[]
  . If `last_block_len = 0`, the final block is empty or has already been processed: the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>).
  . If `index` > `MAX_BLOCKS`, then the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than the AD may hold.
  . `offset <- offset xor L*`.
  . `tmp <- ocb_pad(INPUT, last_block_len) xor offset`.
  . `hash_A <- hash_A xor enc_blk(key, tmp)`.
  . `last_block_len <- 0`.
--
+
An empty final block of the AD, set with `last_block_len` = 0, needs no `kl.exec` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>): the message continues with the `kl.setst` into _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_.
A `kl.setst` that leaves _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ before its `kl.exec` omits the final block of the AD.

** On entering State _Encrypt_ or _Decrypt_, by a Form A `kl.setst`, the Machine computes the nonce-dependent variables of cite:[RFC7253] {sect}4.2:
+
--
 * `Nonce_be : bits(128)` – this is `Nonce` in the RFC, in the big-endian view.
 * `Ktop : bits(128)`, `Ktop_be : bits(128)`
 * `Stretch_be : bits(192)` – this is `Stretch` in the RFC, in the big-endian view.
 * `bottom : int`

[]
 . `Nonce_be <- bin(tag_len mod 128, 7) @ zeros(120 - N_len) @ 1 @ nonce_be(N, N_len)`.
 . `bottom <- int(Nonce_be[5:0])`.
 . `Ktop <- enc_blk(key, bswap(Nonce_be[127:6] @ zeros(6)))`.
 . `Ktop_be <- bswap(Ktop)`.
 . `Stretch_be <- Ktop_be @ (Ktop_be[127:64] xor Ktop_be[119:56])`.
 . `index <- 1`.
 . `offset <- bswap(Stretch_be[191-bottom : 64-bottom])`.
--

** In State _Encrypt_, (multi-block) `kl.exec` instructions are expected to be of Form A, absorbing the plaintext `INPUT` into `checksum_P` and encrypting it into `OUTPUT` as follows:
+
--
 . If `index` > `MAX_BLOCKS`, then the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than the plaintext may hold.
 . `offset <- offset xor L[ntz(index)]`.
 . `checksum_P <- checksum_P xor INPUT`.
 . `OUTPUT <- offset xor enc_blk(key, INPUT xor offset)`.
 . `index <- index + 1`.
--
** When entering State _Enc_Last_Block_:
 . A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_enc_last_block` and auxiliary operand `Xs` is expected, where `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `0` {le} `Xs` {le} `120` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
 . `last_block_len <- Xs`, the bit length of the final block of the plaintext.
+
Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

** {empty}
+
--
In State _Enc_Last_Block_, if `last_block_len` is nonzero, a single Form A
`kl.exec` is expected, which performs:

 * `tmp : bits(b)`

[]
 . If `index` > `MAX_BLOCKS`, then the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than the plaintext may hold.
 . `offset <- offset xor L*`.
 . `tmp[last_block_len-1:0] <- enc_blk(key, offset)[last_block_len-1:0]`. // PAD in RFC{nbsp}7253
 . `OUTPUT <- zeros(128-last_block_len) @ (INPUT[last_block_len-1:0] xor tmp[last_block_len-1:0])`.
 . `tmp <- offset xor ocb_pad(INPUT, last_block_len)`.
 . `checksum_P <- enc_blk(key, checksum_P xor tmp xor L$) xor hash_A`. // tag

If `last_block_len` is zero, a single Form D `kl.exec` is expected instead,
which performs:

. `checksum_P <- enc_blk(key, checksum_P xor offset xor L$) xor hash_A`.

In both cases the state then transitions to _Enc_Tag_Finalize_.
--

** In State _Enc_Tag_Finalize_, a single Form C `kl.exec` is expected.
   It performs `OUTPUT <- zeros(b-tag_len) @ checksum_P[tag_len-1:0]`, and the state transitions to _Success_.
   A `KLLEN` smaller than `tag_len` violates the granularity of the State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).

** In State _Decrypt_, (multi-block) ``kl.exec`` instructions are expected to be of Form A.
   Such a ``kl.exec`` decrypts `INPUT` into `OUTPUT`, and absorbs the
   plaintext `OUTPUT` into `checksum_P`, namely:
+
--
  * `tmp : bits(b)`

[]
  . If `index` > `MAX_BLOCKS`, then the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than the ciphertext may hold.
  . `offset <- offset xor L[ntz(index)]`.
  . `tmp <- offset xor dec_blk(key, INPUT xor offset)`.
  . `checksum_P <- checksum_P xor tmp`.
  . `OUTPUT <- tmp`.
  . `index <- index + 1`.
--
** When entering State _Dec_Last_Block_:
 . A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_dec_last_block` and auxiliary operand `Xs` is expected, where `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `0` {le} `Xs` {le} `120` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
 . `last_block_len <- Xs`, the bit length of the final block of the ciphertext.
+
Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

** In State _Dec_Last_Block_, if `last_block_len` is nonzero, a single Form A
`kl.exec` is expected, which performs:
+
--
   * `tmp : bits(b)`

[]
   . If `index` > `MAX_BLOCKS`, then the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_: the block would be one more than the ciphertext may hold.
   . `offset <- offset xor L*`.
   . `tmp[last_block_len-1:0] <- enc_blk(key, offset)[last_block_len-1:0]`. // PAD in RFC{nbsp}7253
   . `OUTPUT <- zeros(128-last_block_len) @ (INPUT[last_block_len-1:0] xor tmp[last_block_len-1:0])`.
   . `tmp <- offset xor ocb_pad(OUTPUT, last_block_len)`.
   . `checksum_P <- enc_blk(key, checksum_P xor tmp xor L$) xor hash_A`. // tag

If `last_block_len` is zero, a single Form D `kl.exec` is expected instead,
which performs:

. `checksum_P <- enc_blk(key, checksum_P xor offset xor L$) xor hash_A`.

In both cases the state then transitions to _Hash_Verify_.
--

** In State _Hash_Verify_, a single Form B `kl.exec` completes decryption (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>): it compares the `tag_len` least significant bits of
   `INPUT` with those of `checksum_P`. If they match, the state transitions to _Success_; otherwise, it
   transitions to _Failure_. A `KLLEN` smaller than `tag_len` violates the granularity of the State (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).


//

Code examples for OCB encryption and decryption are given in <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-OCB-mode-examples>>.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-OCB-with-nonce-mode]]
=== OCB with Set Nonce

The mode of operation “OCB with Set Nonce” is a variant of OCB (<<KLE-OCB-mode>>) in which the nonce is
carried in the Provisioning Input, as the IV is in <<KLE-GCM-with-IV-mode>>.
This Machine is the same as OCB, its Parameters and Machine-Specific Functions included, except as follows.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[cols="^8%,<15%,<15%,<62%",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field       | Description
|  ii   | `k` or 64     | `key`       | Key value or System Key Identifier
| iii   | 120           | `N`         | The nonce, in the format of the `INPUT` of the _Set_Aux_Value_ `kl.exec` of <<KLE-OCB-mode>>.
|  iv   | 7             | `N_len`     | The bit length of the nonce.
|   v   | 1             | Padding     | {empty}
|===
--

* *Internal State*: {empty}
+
--
Same as for OCB.

Provisioning sets `N_len` from the PI, and sets `N` from the PI as the _Set_Aux_Value_ `kl.exec` of <<KLE-OCB-mode>> sets it from its `INPUT`.
An `N_len` outside [6, 120], in the PI or in an imported Serialized Content, causes the locker to transition to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
--

* *Serialized Content*: {empty}
+
--
Same as for OCB.
--

State Machine::

* *States and Allowed Transitions:* {empty}
+
--
Same as for OCB, with the following exceptions:

** There is no State _Set_Aux_Value_. The transition is from _Ready_ to _Hash_Absorb_.
--

* *State Machine Behavior:* {empty}
+
--
The differences with respect to OCB are:

 ** The Form B `kl.setst` that sets `tag_len` and transitions to State _Hash_Absorb_ is issued in State _Ready_.
 ** This Machine does not use `HasN`: bit 0 of _StateExtension_ stays 0. Entering State _Hash_Absorb_ requires no `HasN`: every provisioning and import supplies `N` and `N_len`.
 ** Entering State _Ready_ clears neither `N` nor `N_len`: a later message uses the same nonce. Software that needs another nonce provisions another locker.
--

This Machine is not a `kl.derive` destination (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>): its key and its nonce are configured together, by provisioning or import.

[NOTE]
====
This Machine protects the confidentiality of the nonce, not against its reuse (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>).
A key that software knows, derived into the key field while the nonce is kept, would let it recover the nonce.
So would the same key in another locker that software can use, if that locker:

* computes the inverse block cipher under the key, such as a decrypting locker of <<KLE-ECB-mode>>: with it, software recovers the nonce from the 128-bit tags of two messages of this Machine;
* computes the block cipher under the key, such as an encrypting locker of <<KLE-ECB-mode>> or a locker of <<KLE-keystream-modes>>: with it, software tests guesses of the nonce; or
* takes a nonce that software chooses, such as a locker of <<KLE-OCB-mode>>, whose nonce software sets in _Set_Aux_Value_, or a locker of this Machine provisioned with a guessed nonce: with it, software also tests guesses of the nonce.

Software holds the same key in another locker when it can provision, by its SKID, a System Key that the SKS allows for that locker's Machine.
====

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-CMAC-mode]]
=== CMAC Functions

{empty}
(((KLE, Machine, CMAC)))
CMAC is a message authentication code built on a block cipher.
Tetsu Iwata and Kaoru Kurosawa designed it, and it became a NIST
Recommendation in May 2005 cite:[nist-SP-800-38B].

Parameters::

* `b` = 64 or 128, the block size of the underlying block cipher (cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}5.2).
* `k` = key size (8|`k`).
* Granularity: `b`.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|  ii  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
|===
--

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits.
 ** `hash`: `b` bits.
 ** `last_block_len`: an integer in the range 0 to `b`, serialized on 32 bits.
 ** `tag_len`: the number of bits that _Hash_Verify_ compares, an integer in the range 64 to `b`, or 0 before it is set; serialized on 32 bits.
 ** `absorbed_any`: a boolean, set once a block has been absorbed in _Hash_Absorb_.
 ** `absorbed_all`: a boolean, set once the last block has been absorbed.

* *MDH: MachinePolicy*: bit 0 permits generating a CMAC (_Hash_Output_), and bit 1 verifying one (_Hash_Verify_); at least one bit must be set (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).


* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)   | Field
|    i  | `k` or 64     | `key` or System Key Identifier
|   ii  | `b`           | `hash`
|  iii  | 32            | `last_block_len`
|   iv  | 32            | `tag_len`
|    v  | 1 (boolean)   | `absorbed_any`
|   vi  | 1 (boolean)   | `absorbed_all`
|===
--

//

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `enc_blk(key, p) -> c` encrypts block `p` using the key `key`, and returns the result `c`.
* `double(S)` = `bswap(dbl(bswap(S)))`, the doubling of cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}6.1, where
**  `dbl(V)` = `V << 1` if `V[b-1]` = 0, else `(V << 1) xor (zeros(b-8) @ R~b~)`, with +
`R~b~` the constant of {sect}5.3: `R~128~` = `0b10000111` (`x^128^ + x^7^ + x^2^ + x + 1`) for `b` = 128, and +
`R~64~` = `0b00011011` (`x^64^ + x^4^ + x^3^ + x + 1`) for `b` = 64.
** For every block cipher with `b` = 128, SM4 included, `dbl` is the `update_mask` of `AES128_XEX` (<<KLE-XEX-XTS-modes>>),
  and `double` is the OCB3 doubling of <<KLE-OCB-mode>>.
* `gen_subkeys(K) -> (K1,K2)` generates subkeys `K1` and `K2` from `K` as cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}6.1 does:

 . `L <- enc_blk(K, zeros(b))`
 . `K1 <- double(L)`
 . `K2 <- double(K1)`
 . return `(K1, K2)`.


State Machine::

* *States:*
 ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Absorb data into the CMAC function.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_: Set the length of the last block to absorb, then absorb it.
 ** _Hash_Output_: Output the CMAC.
 ** _Hash_Verify_: Compare the CMAC with a value supplied by software, without disclosing it.
 ** _Success_: The CC has completed the CMAC operation.
 ** _Failure_: A CMAC verification has failed.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Hash_Absorb_ -> _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ -> _Hash_Output_ -> _Success_.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ -> _Hash_Verify_ -> _Success_ or _Failure_.
 ** From any valid state to State _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** Entering State _Ready_:
  *** `last_block_len <- 0`
  *** `tag_len <- 0`
  *** `hash <- zeros(b)`
  *** `absorbed_any <- false`
  *** `absorbed_all <- false`

 ** When entering State _Hash_Absorb_, the `kl.setst` is of Form A.

 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_, a Form B `kl.exec`, which may span several blocks, absorbs each `b`-bit block `P` of `INPUT` in turn (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>):
+
--
   . `hash <- enc_blk(key, hash xor P)`.
   . `absorbed_any <- true`.
--
** When entering State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, the `kl.setst` is of Form B, with immediate `kl_state_hash_last_block` and auxiliary operand `Xs`, the bit length of the last block:
+
--
. it sets `last_block_len <- Xs`;
. `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with `Xs` {le} `b` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>), and `Xs` = 0, which denotes the empty message, requires `absorbed_any` = `false`; otherwise the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_;
. if `Xs` = 0, the empty final block is absorbed at once, without a `kl.exec` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>>):
.. `(K1, K2) <- gen_subkeys(key)`,
.. `hash <- enc_blk(key, hash xor (zeros(b-8) @ 0b10000000) xor K2)`, and
.. `absorbed_all <- true`.
--

** In State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, a single Form B `kl.exec` absorbs the last block.
    It uses only the `last_block_len` least significant bits of `INPUT` and ignores the others.
+
--
  . `(K1, K2) <- gen_subkeys(key)`.
  . if `last_block_len` = `b`, then:
    .. `tmp <- hash xor INPUT[b-1:0] xor K1`
  . else, that is if `0` < `last_block_len` < `b`:
    .. `tmp <- hash xor` +
`(zeros(b - 8 - last_block_len) @ 0x80 @ INPUT[last_block_len-1:0]) xor K2`
  . `hash <- enc_blk(key, tmp)`
  . `absorbed_all <- true`

A `kl.exec` with `absorbed_all` = `true`, or a `KLLEN` smaller than `last_block_len` (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>), transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
--

** A Form A `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_output` enters State _Hash_Output_.
+
A `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Output_ with `absorbed_all` = `false` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

** In State _Hash_Output_, a single Form C `kl.exec` outputs the CMAC:
+
--
. `OUTPUT <- hash`, with the bits beyond the `b`-th cleared (Rule <<KLE-MGR-clear-output>>); with `KLLEN` < `b`, it emits the first `KLLEN`/8 bytes, the truncated tag of cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}6.2.
. The CC transitions to State _Success_.

The `kl.exec` processes a single block, so its only interruption point is 0 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>).

A `KLLEN` smaller than 64 transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).
--

** State _Hash_Output_, as a `kl.derive` source (<<KLE-derive-endpoints>>), supplies the `b`/8 bytes of `hash`.

** A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_verify` and auxiliary operand `Xs` enters State _Hash_Verify_:
+
--
. `Xs` must be a multiple of 8 with 64 {le} `Xs`; else it transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. It enters State _Hash_Verify_ and sets `tag_len <- min(Xs, b)`.

A `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Verify_ with `absorbed_all` = `false` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
--

** In State _Hash_Verify_, a single Form B `kl.exec` verifies the tag:
+
--
. It compares `INPUT[tag_len-1:0]` with `hash[tag_len-1:0]` in constant time (Rule <<KLE-MGR-tag-verify>>).
`hash[tag_len-1:0]` is the first `tag_len`/8 bytes of the CMAC, MSB~Tlen~ of cite:[nist-SP-800-38B] {sect}6.2.
. The CC transitions to State _Success_ if they match, else to _Failure_.

A `KLLEN` smaller than `tag_len` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-granularity>>).
--

//

Code examples are given in <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-CMAC-mode-example>>.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs]]
=== Hash and MAC Functions, and XOFs

{empty}
(((KLE, Machine, general hashing)))
(((KLE, Machine, MAC functions)))
(((KLE, Machine, XOF)))
This section defines an interface for hash functions, MAC functions and XOFs.
No interface fits every construction, but the one defined here covers the most common, such as SHA-2 cite:[nist-fips-180-4], SHA-3, and SHAKE cite:[nist-fips-202].

Every Machine that instantiates it pads the message as its referenced standard prescribes:
the caller supplies the message alone, and the `kl.setst` that ends the message completes the
padding and applies any length encoding of that standard.
One instruction sequence thus serves all of them (<<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-hash-example>>).

It is highly recommended that a new Machine for a hash function, MAC function or XOF work with the same instruction sequence, unless a reason prevents it.

Parameters::

* `b` = block size (8|`b`): the size of an absorbed block, which may differ from the size of the internal state. For a sponge function, `b` is the _rate_.
* `t` = output block length (8|`t`): the size of the digest, or of each block that the output stage emits between two `update()`. This description assumes `t` {le} `b`.
* `d` = digest length (8|`d`) of a fixed-output Machine: `t`, unless the Machine states otherwise, as <<KLE-Ascon-Hash256>> does. An XOF has no `d`.
* `n` = size of the internal state kept by the function (8|`n`). For a sponge function, `n-b` is the _capacity_ of the function.
* `k` = key size (8|`k`). Present only if keyed, i.e., a MAC.
* `state_offset` = offset into the state at which the block is absorbed.
* `tag_offset` = offset of the tag into the state, 0 unless the Machine states otherwise.
  A Machine must satisfy `state_offset` {le} `tag_offset` and `tag_offset` + `t` {le} `state_offset` + `b` for a sponge, so that every output bit comes from the rate,
  and `tag_offset` + `t` {le} `n` otherwise.
* `g` = granularity: 8 bits in _Hash_Absorb_ and _Hash_Output_, for every instantiation; a State that loads a field, such as _Set_Key_ of <<KLE-HMAC>>, uses it too unless the Machine states otherwise.

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*:
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits)             | Field
.2+|  ii  | If keyed: `k` or 64  | `key` or System Key Identifier
<|          If unkeyed: 0          | none: the Provisioning Input is the MDH only
|===
--

* *MDH: MachinePolicy*: unless the instantiating Machine states otherwise, no State is gated by _MachinePolicy_
  (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>), and a non-zero value is unsupported Metadata (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).

* *Internal State*:
 ** `key`: `k` bits. Present only if keyed, i.e., a MAC.
 ** `state`: `n` bits.
 ** `last_block_len`: int. Bit length of the final piece to be absorbed in _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_.
 ** `input_base`/`output_base` (same field): int. Offset within the input or output at which to read or write.
 ** `block_base`: int. Length of the data absorbed so far into the current block or, in _Hash_Output_, emitted so far from the current `t`-bit output block.
 ** `out_len`: int. In _Hash_Output_, the number of output bits still to be emitted, or 0 for an XOF without bound; zero in every other State.
 ** `cumul_len`: int. Cumulative length of the data absorbed so far.
 ** `block`: `b` bits. Present only if a partial block is kept separately, for instance when inputs are not absorbed directly into `state`.

* *Serialized Content*:
+
--
[cols="^6%,<21%,<18%,<55%",options="header"]
|===
|  Pos. | Size (Bits)          | Field             | Notes
.2+| i  | If keyed: `k` or 64  | `key` or SKID     | --
<|        If unkeyed: 0        | none              | --
|   ii | `n`                  | `state`           | --
|  iii | 16                   | `last_block_len`    | The bit length of the final piece still to be absorbed, 0 once the message ends; in _Hash_Output_, `out_len` where the Machine says so, as <<KLE-BLAKE2>> does.
|   iv | 16                    | `block_base`     | Amount of data, in bits, stored so far into `block` or accumulated (usually XORed) into `state`; in _Hash_Output_, the amount emitted from the current output block.
| v, vi | 32                   | `out_len`         | Unless the Machine uses these bits otherwise, as <<KLE-BLAKE2>> does.
|   vii | 64                   | `cumul_len`       | Cumulative length of the data absorbed so far; its width limits the length of a message (Rule <<KLE-MGR-caller-limits>>). Present only if the padding or another function of the Machine uses it, or under HMAC (<<KLE-HMAC>>).
|  viii | `b`                  | `block`           | Only if the block is stored separately from the `state`.
|===

`input_base`/`output_base` is not serialized: it is set to 8 {interpunct} `klstart` at the start of every `kl.exec`.
--

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `process_block()`: Process `block` into the hash or MAC.
* `finalize()`: Apply to the absorbed message the padding, and any length encoding or final-block processing, of
  the referenced standard, and process `state`, in place, so that it holds the digest, or the first `t` bits of
  output, at `tag_offset`.
* `update()`: Update `state` during the output stage.
  A fixed-output Machine with `d` {le} `t` needs none: its output loop enters _Success_ and returns before it reaches `update()`.

State Machine::

* *States:* _Ready_, _Hash_Absorb_, _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, _Hash_Output_ and _Success_.

 ** _Ready_: The CC is ready.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_: Absorb the message into the hash function.
 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_: Set the bit length of the final piece of the message, then absorb it.
 ** _Hash_Output_: Output the hash.
 ** _Success_: The CC has completed the operation (an XOF reaches it only with a bounded output).

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** _Ready_ -> _Hash_Absorb_ -> { _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ -> } _Hash_Output_ -> _Success_.
 ** The Machine makes no transition into _Hash_Output_, or into the _Hash_Verify_ of a MAC, by itself: a `kl.setst` naming it ends the message.
 ** From any valid state to State _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior:*
 ** Upon entering State _Ready_:
  *** `last_block_len`, `block_base`, `cumul_len`, `out_len` and `block` are set to zero.
  *** `state`: the initial value is set as specified by the Machine.

 ** The `kl.setst` that enters _Hash_Absorb_ is of Form A, unless the Machine states otherwise.
    It sets `last_block_len` {leftarrow} `b` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>).

 ** State _Hash_Absorb_ invokes macro `PROCESS_VLI` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>) as:
+
`{fournbsp}PROCESS_VLI(0, block, b, state, n, input_base, block_base,` +
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp} state_offset, cumul_len, process_block(), None, mode, 8,` +
`{fournbsp}{fournbsp} Hash_Absorb_Last_Block, None, last_block_len)`,
+
where:
+
--
** `state_offset` is Machine-defined;
** `cumul_len` is `None` where neither the Machine nor <<KLE-HMAC>> uses it;
** `finalize()` is `None` in this invocation: it is performed once, by the `kl.setst` that ends the message;
** `mode` is:
*** `assign` for a construction that fills a separate `block` and then processes it into `state` through `process_block()`, and
*** `xor_accumulate` for one that absorbs the input directly into `state`, as a sponge does; each instantiation states which it uses.
--
+
A `kl.exec` thus absorbs any whole number of bytes. Its interruption points are those of <<KLE-process-VLI>>: 0 and, if the locker holds `block_base` = 0 when it is issued, every byte offset.

 ** State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, entered from _Hash_Absorb_:
+
--
. A Form A `kl.setst` keeps `last_block_len` = `b` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>).
. A Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_hash_last_block` and auxiliary operand `Xs` sets `last_block_len <- Xs`. `Xs` must satisfy:
.. 0 {le} `Xs` {le} `b`;
.. `Xs` is a multiple of 8, unless the Machine admits other bit lengths as Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>> permits.
. Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
. If `last_block_len` = 0, nothing more happens: the final piece is empty, and the message is what _Hash_Absorb_ absorbed.
No `kl.exec` is needed; one whose transfer window is not empty transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rules <<KLE-MGR-single-last-block>> and <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-transfer-window-checks>>).
. If `last_block_len` {ne} 0, a single Form B `kl.exec` absorbs the final piece as <<KLE-process-VLI>> specifies for `last_block_state`:
.. unless the Machine states otherwise, the piece is the `last_block_len` least significant bits of `INPUT`;
.. its only interruption point is 0 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-one-klexec-per-block>>).
--

 ** From _Hash_Absorb_, or from _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ with `last_block_len` = 0, the `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Output_ ends the message:
+
--
. Issued from _Hash_Absorb_, it gives the result of an empty final piece followed by the same `kl.setst`.
. It is of Form A or of Form B, unless the Machine requires only one Form.
. A Form B auxiliary operand `Xs` {ne} 0 is the output length in bits, and must:
.. be at most `d` for a fixed-output Machine, and below 2^32^ for an XOF;
.. be a multiple of 8, unless the Machine states otherwise, as <<KLE-KMAC>> does for KMAC128 and KMAC256;
.. be at least the minimum that a MAC may set, as <<KLE-HMAC>> does with 64 bits and <<KLE-KMAC>> with 32 bits for KMAC128 and KMAC256.

Any other non-zero `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_, before `finalize()`.
--
+
In _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, `last_block_len` {ne} 0 means that the final piece is not yet absorbed: a `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Output_, or the _Hash_Verify_ of a MAC, then transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.

 ** In State _Hash_Output_:
 *** Upon entering the state, the following operations are first performed:
 . `finalize()`
 . `block_base <- 0` and `last_block_len <- 0`
 . `out_len <- Xs` for a Form B `kl.setst` with `Xs` {ne} 0; otherwise `out_len <- d` for a fixed-output Machine, and `out_len <- 0` for an XOF.
*** For each Form C `kl.exec` instruction, the
    following operations are performed:
+
--
    * `amount : int`, `{nbsp}` amount of data to copy from `state` to `OUTPUT`

[]
   . `output_base <- 8 {interpunct} klstart` // 0 when the instruction starts
    . while (`output_base` < `KLLEN`) do:
     .. `amount <- min(KLLEN - output_base, t - block_base)`, and, if `out_len` {ne} 0, `amount <- min(amount, out_len)`
     .. ``OUTPUT[output_base + amount - 1 : output_base] <- `` +
           ``{fournbsp}state[tag_offset + block_base + amount - 1 : tag_offset + block_base]``
     .. `output_base <- output_base + amount`
     .. `block_base <- block_base + amount`
     .. if `out_len` {ne} 0 then:
      ... `out_len <- out_len - amount`
      ... if `out_len` = 0, any bits of `OUTPUT` beyond `output_base` are cleared,
          the state transitions to _Success_, and the instruction returns.
     .. if (`block_base` = `t`) then:
      ... `update()`
      ... `block_base <- 0`
      ... *Here*, and only here, the instruction can be interrupted while `output_base` < `KLLEN`, with `klstart <- output_base / 8`.
          A halt here leaves `block_base` = 0.
--
+
The output thus has as many bits as `out_len` holds on entering the State, or is unbounded for an XOF without bound, and is emitted in any whole number of bytes.
+
The interruption points of `kl.exec` in _Hash_Output_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-interruption-point>>) are 0 and, if the locker holds `block_base` = 0 when the `kl.exec` is issued, any byte offset, whatever `out_len`.

The following are Machine-specific, and the code example of <<Zkl-software.adoc#KLE-pseudocode-hash>> uses none of them:

* the parameters `nn` and `kk` of <<KLE-BLAKE2>>, set by an operation in _Ready_;
* the customization string of <<KLE-Ascon-CXOF128>>, absorbed in _Set_Aux_Value_;
* the key of the NIK variant of <<KLE-HMAC>>, loaded in _Set_Key_;
* the verification of a MAC in _Hash_Verify_ (<<KLE-HMAC>>, <<KLE-KMAC>>).

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-SHA-2]]
=== SHA-2 Family Specific Rules

{empty}
(((KLE, Machine, SHA-2)))
The primitives of the SHA-2 family cite:[nist-fips-180-4] — SHA-224, SHA-256, SHA-384,
SHA-512, SHA-512/224, and SHA-512/256 — are instantiations of the algorithm specified in
<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, and only the differences with respect to the behavior described
there are given here.

Parameters::

* The word size `w`, and `b`, `n`, and `t`, as given in <<KLE-SHA-2-parameters>>; the
  internal state consists of the eight `w`-bit chaining variables of
  cite:[nist-fips-180-4].
// Before byte-granular hash inputs:
// * Granularity: 32 bits. It constrains no `KLLEN`, neither in _Hash_Absorb_, since `max_len` = 0 (<<KLE-process-VLI>>), nor in
//   _Hash_Output_, which emits any whole number of bytes (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>). It bounds the
//   `length` of a `kl.derive` of a shared secret (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-checks>> item 5), and sets the interruption points
//   of _Hash_Absorb_ (<<KLE-process-VLI>>).

[[KLE-SHA-2-parameters]]
.Parameters of the SHA-2 family
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="<,^,^,^,^",options="header"]
|===
| Function    | `w` | `b`  | `n`  | `t`
| SHA-224     |  32 |  512 |  256 | 224
| SHA-256     |  32 |  512 |  256 | 256
| SHA-384     |  64 | 1024 |  512 | 384
| SHA-512     |  64 | 1024 |  512 | 512
| SHA-512/224 |  64 | 1024 |  512 | 224
| SHA-512/256 |  64 | 1024 |  512 | 256
|===


Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*: the MDH only (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, unkeyed).

* *Internal State*:

   ** `state`: `n` bits, holding the eight chaining variables `H~i~` big-endian:
      `state[(i+1)w-1 : iw]` = `bswap(bin(H~i~, w))`, for `0` {le} `i` < 8.
   ** `block` (`b` bits).
   ** `block_base`, `last_block_len` and `out_len` (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).
   ** `cumul_len`, the number of message bits absorbed.

* *Serialized Content*: as prescribed by <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, with `out_len` in Positions v and vi,
  and `cumul_len` and `block` present.

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `process_block()`: applies the compression function of cite:[nist-fips-180-4] {sect}6 for the
  selected function to absorb the current `block` into `state`, message word `j` being
  `int(bswap(block[(j+1)w-1 : jw]))`, for `0` {le} `j` < 16.
* `finalize()`: absorbs the rest of the padding of cite:[nist-fips-180-4] {sect}5.1.1 and {sect}5.1.2 for a message
  of `ℓ` bits, by steps 3.a to 3.e and 3.g of <<KLE-process-VLI>>, so that `cumul_len` does not count it.
  This rest is the byte `0x80` if `cumul_len` is a multiple of 8 (otherwise the final piece carries the bit 1 already),
  then zero bytes until `block_base` = `b` − 2`w`, then `bswap(bin(ℓ, 2w))`.
  `ℓ` is `cumul_len`, or, under HMAC, the length that <<KLE-HMAC>> states.

State Machine::

* *States and Allowed State Transitions:* those of <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>.

* *Behavior:*
 ** Upon entering State _Ready_, `state` is set to the initial hash value of the selected function
    per cite:[nist-fips-180-4] {sect}5.3, and the fields of <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>> are zeroed.
 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_, `PROCESS_VLI` is invoked as in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, with `mode` = `assign`,
    `state_offset` = 0 and `cumul_len` tracked.
+
NOTE: `mode` is `assign` because the SHA-2 family fills a separate `block` and compresses it into `state`.
 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_:
+
--
. The final piece is the {lceil}``last_block_len``/8{rceil} least significant bytes of `INPUT`; the other bits of `INPUT` are ignored.
. Let `q` = `last_block_len` mod 8. If `q` {ne} 0:
.. the last of these bytes carries the final `q` bits of the piece in its `q` most significant positions, since cite:[nist-fips-180-4] orders the bits of a byte from the most significant (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>, second bullet);
.. the Machine replaces the other 8 − `q` bits of that byte by the bit 1 that starts the padding, followed by zeros.
. The `kl.exec` of `last_block_state` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>) absorbs these bytes: its step 3, with all sub-steps, uses 8 {interpunct} {lceil}``last_block_len``/8{rceil} in place of `last_block_len`.
. In place of the increments of step 3.f of the `kl.exec` in _Current_State_, `cumul_len` advances once by `last_block_len` itself, before step 5 clears `last_block_len`.
--
 ** In State _Hash_Output_, the bytes emitted are the chaining variables in the big-endian encoding,
    truncated to the number of bits that `out_len` holds on entering the State (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-SM3]]
=== SM3 Specific Rules

{empty}
(((KLE, Machine, SM3)))
The ShangMi 3 hash function, standardized as GB/T 32905-2016 cite:[GBT-32905-2016], follows the rules
of the SHA-2 family (<<KLE-SHA-2>>) with `w` = 32, `b` = 512, `n` = 256, and `t` = 256.
It has the big-endian representation, padding rule, word mapping, `state` layout and state machine of SHA-256.
Its initial hash value and compression function are those of the SM3 standard.

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-BLAKE2]]
=== BLAKE2 Specific Rules

{empty}
(((KLE, Machine, BLAKE2)))
BLAKE2b and BLAKE2s cite:[RFC7693], unkeyed or keyed, instantiate <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>; only the
differences are given here.
`nn` and `kk` are the digest and key lengths in bytes.

Parameters::

* `w`, `r` (the rounds of `F`), `b`, `n`, `nn~max~`, `kk~max~` and the rotation constants of `G`, as in
  <<KLE-BLAKE2-parameters>>; `t` = 8 {interpunct} `nn`.

[[KLE-BLAKE2-parameters]]
.Parameters of BLAKE2
[%autowidth,float="center",align="center",cols="<,^,^,^,^,^,^,^",options="header"]
|===
| Function | `w` | `r` | `b`  | `n` | `nn~max~` | `kk~max~` | (`R1`, `R2`, `R3`, `R4`)
| BLAKE2b  |  64 |  12 | 1024 | 512 |    64     |    64     | (32, 24, 16, 63)
| BLAKE2s  |  32 |  10 |  512 | 256 |    32     |    32     | (16, 12, 8, 7)
|===

Data Structures::

* *Provisioning Input*: the MDH only, or, with a system key (_KeyType_ = 1):
+
--
[%autowidth,cols="^1,<2,<3",options="header"]
|===
| Pos. | Size (Bits) | Field
|  ii  | 64          | System Key Identifier
|===

`kk` is the length of the key that the SKS returns; a length outside 1 to `kk~max~` transitions the locker to Error State
_Invalid_ (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-system-keys>>). When an import resolves the SKID again, a key whose length differs
from the restored `kk` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
_KeyType_ = 2 is invalid: the Machine holds no key by value.
A caller with its own key absorbs it (see below).
--

* *MDH: MachinePolicy*: without a system key, as in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>.
  With a system key, the Machine is a MAC that only generates tags: bit 0 enables _Hash_Output_
  (Rule <<KLE-MGR-policy-gate>>); _MachinePolicy_ = 0 is invalid Metadata, and any set bit other than bit 0 is unsupported Metadata
  (<<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-Metadata-validity>>).

* *Internal State*:
 ** `nn`, with 1 {le} `nn` {le} `nn~max~`, and `kk`, with `kk` {le} `kk~max~`; an import that writes a value outside
    these ranges transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_ (Rule <<KLE-MGR-check-on-write>>).
 ** `state`: the words `h~i~` of cite:[RFC7693], little-endian: `state[(i+1)w-1 : iw]` = `bin(h~i~, w)`, for `0` {le} `i` < 8.
 ** `block`, `block_base`, `last_block_len`, `out_len`, and `cumul_len`, which also counts a key block, so that
    `cumul_len`/8 is the counter of cite:[RFC7693] {sect}3.2.
 ** `syskey`: with a system key, the key that the SKID resolves to, zero-padded to `b` bits. It is not serialized: the
    SKID in Position i of the Serialized Content stands for it (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>).

* *Serialized Content*: as in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, with `cumul_len` and `block` present, and:
 ** the SKID, if any, in Position i;
 ** `nn` in Position v and `kk` in Position vi, 16 bits each, `nn` in the lower half;
 ** in _Hash_Output_, `out_len` in Position iii.

Machine-Specific Functions::

* `F(f)`: the compression function of cite:[RFC7693] {sect}3.2 on `state`, with message words
  `m~j~` = `int(block[(j+1)w-1 : jw])`, the counter `cumul_len`/8, and the final-block flag `f`.
* `process_block()`: `F(false)`.
* `finalize()` performs the first of the following that applies, then sets `block_base` {leftarrow} 0:
** with `block_base` > 0: zeroes `block[b-1 : block_base]` and performs `F(true)`;
** with `block_base` = 0, which only an empty message leaves, and a system key: `block` {leftarrow} `syskey`,
   `cumul_len` {leftarrow} `b`, `F(true)` and `block` {leftarrow} `zeros(b)`;
** with `block_base` = 0, no system key and `kk` = 0: `F(true)` on the zero `block`;
** with `block_base` = 0, no system key and `kk` {ne} 0: the locker transitions to Error State _Invalid_, since
   the caller's key block is missing.

State Machine::

* *States:* _Ready_, _Hash_Absorb_, _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_, _Hash_Output_, and _Success_.

* *Allowed State Transitions:*
 ** As in <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>; the `kl.setst` naming _Hash_Output_ ends the message held in `block`, also
    the empty one.
 ** From any valid state to State _Ready_ (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-to-ready>>).

* *Behavior*, where it differs from <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>:
 ** At the completion of a provisioning, before the operations of entering _Ready_, `nn` {leftarrow} `nn~max~` and
    `kk` {leftarrow} the length of the system key, or 0 without one.
    The completion of a system-specific import does the same for each of them that the converted Content does not
    configure (Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-sysfmt-completion>>).
    Every other entry into _Ready_ keeps `nn` and `kk`.
 ** When entering State _Ready_, `state` is the IV of cite:[RFC7693] {sect}2.6, with `h~0~` XORed with
    `0x01010000` {oplus} `(kk << 8)` {oplus} `nn` ({sect}2.5, {sect}3.3).
 ** In State _Ready_, setting the parameters is an operation, not a State (Rule <<KLE-operation-immediates>>):
+
--
. a Form B `kl.setst` with immediate `kl_state_set_aux_value` and auxiliary operand `Xs` sets `nn` {leftarrow} `Xs[7:0]` and, without a system key,
  `kk` {leftarrow} `Xs[15:8]`, and recomputes `h~0~`;
. `Xs` must satisfy 1 {le} `Xs[7:0]` {le} `nn~max~` and `Xs[15:8]` {le} `kk~max~`, and its bits above bit 15 must
  be 0. With a system key, `Xs[15:8]` must be 0.

Any other `Xs` transitions the locker to Error State _Invalid_.
--
 ** With a system key, the instruction that absorbs the first byte of the message compresses the key block before
    it absorbs that byte: `block` {leftarrow} `syskey`, `cumul_len` {leftarrow} `b`, `F(false)`, and `block`
    {leftarrow} `zeros(b)`.
    That instruction is a `kl.exec` or a `kl.derive` in _Hash_Absorb_, or the `kl.exec` of _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_.
    `cumul_len` = 0 records that the key block is still pending; for the empty message, `finalize()` compresses it
    as the final block.
+
NOTE: `syskey` is in `block` only between two steps of one instruction: the instruction that absorbs the first byte
of the message, or, for the empty message, the `kl.setst` whose `finalize()` compresses the key block. Neither
instruction can halt between these steps, so no Serialized Content holds `syskey`
(Rule <<Zkl-ISA-unpriv.adoc#KLE-GR-skid-field>>).
 ** A caller with its own key, and no system key:
+
--
. sets `kk` to the length of the key in bytes by the _Set_Aux_Value_ operation;
. absorbs the key, zero-padded to `b` bits, as the first block of the message (cite:[RFC7693] {sect}3.3);
. for an empty message, absorbs nothing more, and `finalize()` compresses the key block as the final block.
--
+
NOTE: The Machine does not tell the key block from the message: it checks neither that `kk` matches the length of the key nor that the key is padded with zeros, and a mismatch only yields a different hash.
 ** In State _Hash_Absorb_, `PROCESS_VLI` is invoked with `max_len` = 0, `state_offset` = 0 and `mode` = `assign`, with these differences:
+
--
. Step 3.g is postponed: a `block` filled to `block_base` = `b` is kept.
. The iteration of step 3 that absorbs the next byte of the message first compresses that `block` by `F(false)` and sets `block_base` {leftarrow} 0, before step 3.a, so that the counter `cumul_len`/8 does not yet count that byte.
. With a system key, the key block is compressed first, as stated above.
. The interruption points of `kl.exec` in _Hash_Absorb_ are 0 and, if the locker holds `block_base` = 0 or `block_base` = `b` when the `kl.exec` is issued, any byte offset.
A halt leaves the full block held, with `block_base` = `b`, so that its resumption meets that condition.
--

 ** _Hash_Absorb_Last_Block_ follows <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>, with `Xs` a multiple of 8 (Rule <<KLE-MGR-last-blk-len>>).
In the `kl.exec` of `last_block_state` (<<KLE-process-VLI>>), step 3.c is postponed in the same way:
+
--
. A full block, including one held from _Hash_Absorb_, is compressed by `F(false)`, with `block_base` {leftarrow} 0, at the start of the loop iteration that absorbs the next byte of the final piece, before its `amount` is computed.
. A final piece that fills its block is held for `finalize()`.
--

// ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

[[KLE-HMAC]]
=== HMAC

Hash function Machines suffice to compute HMACs, but an environment may want to hide the HMAC key.
This section extends <<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>> accordingly.

`finalize()` of `H` pads as in `H`; for SHA-2 (<<KLE-SHA-2>>) and SM3 (<<KLE-SM3>>) it encodes `ℓ` = `b` + `cumul_len` bits for the inner hash and `b` + `t` bits for the outer, since `absorb(K0 xor ipad)` and `absorb(K0 xor opad)` are not counted in `cumul_len`.

Parameters::
+
--
* `H` = the underlying hash function.
* `b` = the input block size of `H`: its parameter `b`, which for SHA-3 is the rate (<<KLE-SHA-3-parameters>>).
* `t` = the digest size: the parameter `t` of `H` (<<KLE-hash-functions-MACs-XOFs>>).
* Granularity: same as that of the underlying hash function.

The two padding constants of cite:[nist-fips-198-1] are:

* `ipad` = the byte `0x36` repeated `b`/8 times, and
* `opad` = the byte `0x5c` repeated `b`/8 times,

both `b` bits long. All bytes of each constant are equal, so its
